Price evidence convention used in every table: **L** = listed price read on the seller's own page on 2026-09-27; **A** = distributor price and stock read on 2026-09-27 through the OEMsTrade aggregator page for the part, because Mouser, DigiKey and Newark block automated reads (the owner verifies it in a browser); **E** = estimate with its basis, never a listed price. No page was logged into, no form was filled, nothing was added to a cart and no file was downloaded by the author (datasheets read through the web-fetch tool, which caches the PDF it reads, are named where used).

## 1. Executive summary

- **Owner budget (status note section 10, governs).** Target USD 200 for the first complete radio; **USD 300 is the absolute maximum**, and the worst case including contingency must stay at or below it. **Sales tax is excluded.** Owned and not bought: 24 AWG magnet wire, a 2 m antenna with an SMA-male plug, through-hole resistors and capacitors (surface-mount passives are not owned), a USB power adapter. Answered later the same day (status note section 11): no 18650 cells or charger are owned, so they are priced; test instruments are outside the radio's cap, under a separate USD 300 cap on all new equipment (the tinySA Ultra counts there if it is not yet bought). Revision 5 (status note 2026-09-28 section 1): the tinySA Ultra is not yet bought, and the owner will buy it later; the owner's multimeter is a Fluke 174, which has no temperature input, so a stand-alone K-type thermocouple thermometer is bought inside the equipment cap (status note 2026-09-28 section 2).
- **Recommendation (one sentence, revision 6):** choose **A4 with the TCXO and A5's diode-ring mixer** (300 of 500) over **A5** (270), because the six pre-order analyses removed the evidence on which A5's advantages rested (a module that holds 5 W at the low pack end and a PA bound under 110 C) while confirming its costs (twice the heat in the case, a worst case over USD 300 before guards), and the three assurance conditions of INSP-118 that A4 now carries (TCXO, tab cut-off sensor, PA_EN gating) cost USD 5.19 capped at planning and 15 points net, without reversing the order; that A4 configuration leads A5 by 30 points and in all 16 weight runs between them, and no single Low-cell move closes the gap (section 6). **A2 (305, not analysed) is 5 points above it on the matrix** and is presented with it as the closely ranked alternative, with the reasons it is not recommended and the analysis that would settle it (section 6 revision 6 item 4; Q1).
  - **A4 (revision 6; recommended with the ring):** the revision-1 radio (JFET preamps, 500 Hz crystal ladder, analog audio, Morse menu, Adafruit Si5351A module, the End-of-Life NXP AFT05MS004NT1 with a hand-derived match and a spare in the order, cells charged outside the radio, one Mouser order plus two JLCPCB 2-layer boards), plus the common design items of section 8.14 that the analyses require, plus three conditions from INSP-118 that are now part of A4, not options: **(1) the Epson TG2520SMN TCXO on the Si5351A's XA input and, through a squaring buffer, on a second RP2350 GPIN**, so that REQ-SYS-182 and REQ-SYS-154 hold as written on the frequency-budget route R3 (the former add-back AB2, USD 4.15 capped, now in the baseline; D-17); **(2) the REQ-SYS-181 cut-off sensor NTC-2 on the AFT05 tab copper** (TS-011 rule 14), where it bounds the junction at about 142 C or less against the datasheet's 150 C maximum (D-6); **(3) a PA_EN permit line, independent of TX_KEY, that gates the drive supply and the gate-bias clamp in hardware** together with TX_KEY (D-11, D-18). Scored alone (JFET mixer) it totals 285: the TCXO is the one flagged part (C3 5 to 3). **With A5's diode-ring mixer (U3, USD 2.60 capped) it totals 300** (C5 2 to 3 with the TCXO and the ring together, C2 3 to 2), and it replaces the JFET mixer whose half-IF run is only representative (8 of 16 cases did not converge) with the mixer the receiver analysis showed at 34 to 37 dB margin (section 7.3, table R5-2 row 11). This is the configuration recommended.
  - **A5 ("hybrid H1"):** A4 with the Mitsubishi RA07M1317M module PA, the Epson TG2520SMN TCXO, the diode-ring mixer and a spare GVA-84+, with the same common items; revision 6 adds the same route R3 counter input and PA_EN gating, while its REQ-SYS-181 sensor stays on the sink (it acts there with the channel at about 120 to 123 C against a 175 C rating).
- **What the software assurance pair found (INSP-118, revision 6; section 7.3 and table R6-1):** none of the three Major findings reverses A4 over A5, but each showed the recommended A4 passing a safety requirement its own evidence did not support. (1) REQ-SYS-182: revision 5's budget used a recalled 30 ppm Pico 2 crystal and no counter term; on the reviewed frequency-budget method the RP2350 crystal is 65 ppm and only route R3 (the TCXO counted in receive, which cancels the reference error) meets the 10 kHz limits, with a measured threshold of 5.0 kHz and 482 Hz of margin; A4 without a TCXO would need REQ-SYS-182 widened to at least 16.1 kHz and REQ-SYS-154 to at least 27.7 kHz by CR. The key-down sequence now leaves 7 ms for the frequency check before PA_EN. (2) REQ-SYS-181: on the sink the A4 cut-off never acts; on the tab copper it acts with the junction at about 124 C nominally and at most about 142 C with every input adverse, under the AFT05's 150 C maximum operating junction (NXP datasheet Rev. 0, 7/2014, page 2, Table 1), so no REQ-SYS-181 value change is needed, only the sensing point. (3) REQ-SYS-120: the second condition is now a named PA_EN line, set only by the safe-state manager, which with TX_KEY gates the GVA-84+ supply and the gate-bias clamp in a hardware gate; CLK1 enable is a firmware sequencing action, not a safety condition.
- **What the analyses found (section 7.3, table R5-2; each record and its review verdict in section 9, table R5-1; every figure an estimate):**
  1. **Thermal (WP-PDR-28, `docs/design/analysis/thermal-ts012.md`, reviewer APPROVED).** REQ-SYS-112 as written (110 C, continuous key-down, 45 C) **fails for both**: A4 122.7 C (band +20.3 / -14.7 K), A5 126.0 C (+20.0 / -12.4 K), both with the no-cost design changes of section 8.14; 131.0 and 142.1 C as revision 4 drew them. A5's range (113.6 to 145.9 C) lies wholly over 110 C, so C8 for A5 falls to 1 on the section 3.1 rule; A4's (107.9 to 142.9 C) spans it and stays 2. A5's module case is 105.7 C against the 90 C guidance. A5 puts about twice A4's PA heat in the case: two A5 box criteria fail outside their bands (continuous PETG 66.0 C, long-session cells 58.9 C), none of A4's. Both need a REQ-SYS-112 delta (a duty-limited corner, up to about 60 % duty at 45 C) and a lower REQ-SYS-118 inhibit setpoint on an NTC in the heat path (about 81 C for A5, 77 C for A4). REQ-SYS-113 passes for both only with a wrap-around guard, which adds about +14 mm of length, +4 mm of width and +10 mm of height at the sink end to both (C6 falls to 1 for both).
  2. **PA drive and output power (WP-PDR-21, `pa-drive-ts012.md`, reviewer APPROVED at INSP-114 iteration 3, `f5960d3`).** Neither finalist shows REQ-SYS-012 (3.97 W at the 6.4 V pack end) in any steady key-down case. A5: nominal 4.09 W, lowest corner 3.19 W at 25 C with the filter at its median; with the drain-feed and VGG levers (the note's C2 and C3) -0.17 to -1.21 dB over the key-down cases at the filter median; with every design lever at its best at once -0.45 to +0.84 dB, which needs a 0.5 dB output filter that no LPF build meets (revision 6 wording, INSP-114 X-7). Its fixed drive pad puts the module outside its 10 to 30 mW stability window at 223 of 810 corners; a select-on-test pad closes it only with a level reading shown within +/-1.0 dB. A4: nominal 3.06 W, lowest corner 1.69 W (-3.7 dB), short at every pack voltage; its drive is acceptable. A5 is still 1.3 dB stronger than A4 at the nominal corner, but revision 4's "4.0 to 4.6 W at 6.4 V" for A5 is withdrawn, so C5 for A5 falls from 5 to 4.
  3. **Harmonic LPF (WP-PDR-21, `lpf-ts012.md`, reviewer APPROVED).** Both pass 97.307(e) with the BOM values and catalog coils (A5 7.5 to 9.5 dB; A4 5.5 to 7.5 dB, on an assumed AFT05 harmonic level); A4 needs 2 % parts for the 60 dBc target. No build meets the 0.5 dB passband-loss criterion at the worst case (1.76 dB for the recommended build), which adds to both finalists' REQ-SYS-012 shortfall. A5's binding case (5 W at 8.4 V) is a back-off state outside the module's guarantee.
  4. **Receiver BPF (WP-PDR-19, `rx-bpf-ts012.md` revision 3; INSP-117 iteration 3 NEEDS CHANGES, `e647ab1`; iteration 4 authorized and in progress).** The 2 + 3 resonator filter of revision 4 fails REQ-SYS-033 for both (about 51 dB; revision 4's "well over 90 dB" was wrong). 2 + 3 + 3 met 70 dB by only 0.2 dB at room temperature and is not shown over REQ-SYS-114 (INSP-117 finding-7). **Revision 6: the owner's authorization of INSP-117 iteration 4 adopts 2 + 3 + 4 at IF 8 MHz** (80.1 dB at the design-input corner at room temperature, about 79.4 dB over -10 to +45 C by the reviewer's drift check; residual limit +/-0.97 %), inside row E5 (a). Its four-resonator third section costs MDS: -136.3 dBm (A4) and -134.9 dBm (A5) at the TC-SYS-017 corner (TPM-005 Red there), so the front-end redesign of WP-PDR-19 must recover about 3.7 and 5.1 dB, common to both. Half-IF passes for both (A5's ring 34 to 37 dB margin, A4's JFET 12 to 16 dB, Low). **Revision 8 (INSP-110 finding-26):** superseded by `rx-bpf-ts012.md` revision 4 (`63122e7`; INSP-117 iteration 3 re-issue 1, reviewer APPROVED, `123f048`): 2 + 3 + 4 holds REQ-SYS-033 over REQ-SYS-114 at a worst case of **75.37 dB** (margin 5.37 dB, hot end at 148.0 MHz, +/-0.3 % residual estimate), with an alignment acceptance of **+/-0.62 %** at 20 to 30 C and a limit of **+/-0.64 %** over temperature; the MDS figures stand; no score moves (section 10, revisit (a)).
  5. **Transmit clock spurs (WP-PDR-20, `spurs-ts012.md`, reviewer APPROVED).** Met by wording only for both: with the proposed clock plan and the drive bandpass, 3 lines stay over 25 uW at the high estimate (150.000 MHz and the /8 prescaler kickback). No discrimination; they close only at the tinySA sweep, which waits for the owner's later purchase of the tinySA Ultra.
  6. **Keying envelope (WP-PDR-22, `keying-ts012.md`, reviewer APPROVED at INSP-116 iteration 3, `810850b`).** The loop as revision 4 writes it fails for both. With the revision-1 loop design both pass every element after the first; on the first element of an over A4 passes at the worst-case sum of its estimated budget and A5 does not (its window is 1.6 to 1.8 times narrower). A4 fails REQ-TX-014 (1 uW at key-up) by estimate unless the drive gating becomes part of the key path; revision 6 puts it there in hardware (D-11, D-18), which gives about -67 dBm (A4) and -61 dBm (A5) at key-up by the keying note's path estimate.
- **Cost (section 8.4), sales tax excluded, 15 % contingency on every line, with the analysis-driven parts of row E5 (estimate, USD 5.49 to 19.13 before contingency in revision 6, common to all; it adds item (g), the PA_EN gate, clamp FET and TCXO squaring buffer):** **A5 USD 211.20 low, 248.83 planning, 316.51 worst; A4 with the required TCXO USD 172.31, 205.91, 269.57; A4 with the TCXO and the ring (recommended) USD 174.91, 208.51, 272.17.** A5's worst case is **USD 16.51 over the USD 300 maximum** before the guard ladder: M1 is conditional for A5 (section 4.1). Guards G1 (free Mouser shipping) and G2 bring it to USD 299.83, a margin of USD 0.17; guard G5 (drop the TCXO) is withdrawn, because the TCXO is now a REQ-SYS-182 condition for both finalists. The recommended A4 plans USD 8.51 over the USD 200 target and keeps USD 27.83 of worst-case margin.
- **Risk per dollar (section 8.2, on the section 7.1 scores, revision 6):** A4 now carries six Red risks (the TCXO retires the 30 ppm Red and the hardware drive gating lowers REQ-TX-014 to Yellow); A5 carries eight (seven without the cost Red). The three remaining upgrades from A4 to A5 (module, ring, spare driver; the TCXO is now in both) net **-15 risk points for USD 42.92**, and -31 if A5's cost Red is counted.
- **What the owner gets with the recommended A4:** 5 W at a full pack (nominal corner 5.2 W at 8.4 V) falling to about 3.1 W nominal at the 6.4 V end (lowest corner about 1.7 W), a REQ-SYS-012 delta larger than revision 4 stated; a 500 Hz crystal-ladder receiver with the diode-ring mixer and an MDS near -141 dBm nominal that is not shown at the tolerance corner (the same for A5); the TCXO (REQ-SYS-010 kept, REQ-SYS-182 and REQ-SYS-154 met as written on route R3); an End-of-Life PA with a spare; one flagged part (the leadless TCXO, as A5); half A5's heat in the case; about 161 x 74 x 52 mm at the sink end (estimate) and USD 208.51 planning, 272.17 worst.
- **What A5 would give instead:** about 1.3 dB more output at the nominal corner at the low pack end (still short of REQ-SYS-012), vendor-guaranteed harmonic and stability limits at 6 W and 7.2 V (not at the 5 W, 8.4 V back-off state that binds its harmonic budget), a module in current production, a wider thermal margin on the REQ-SYS-181 cut-off (about 52 K against the module's 175 C channel rating, against about 8 K for A4 at its adverse corner), and an envelope loop that is harder to hold on the first element; for USD 40.32 more at planning, twice the heat in the box and a worst case over the USD 300 maximum before guards.
- **What A2 would give (revision 6, not analysed; section 6 revision 6 item 4):** 305 on the matrix, 5 points above the recommended A4: in-radio USB charging kept (SI-022, CON-010), the TCXO and the diode ring, the best envelope (138 x 67 x 42 mm as its architect drew it), USD 194.19 planning and 255.62 worst. Against that: its C6, C8, C1 and C2 cells are unanalysed estimates of the kind the analyses moved down for both finalists; three flagged parts (C3 1); a new-old-stock PA pair from RF Parts, the RD07MUS2B "no longer available for export"; an LCSC-only charger chain whose JLCPCB merge is unverified; and the Catastrophic HZ-002 charger chain in the box beside the PA.
- **What both give up (section 8.8):** the display (Morse menu instead), in-radio charging, the conductive case coating, the tuning encoder and the stainless SMA jack; continuous key-down at 45 C ambient (REQ-SYS-112 delta to a duty-limited corner); and no transmission on the air before the tinySA sweep, which waits for the owner's tinySA purchase.
- **Problem requiring a decision (one sentence):** which architecture, parts list and requirement set let the owner build the first complete cwht at the owner's home bench within USD 300 worst case (target USD 200), with listed-price parts only.
- **Robustness verdict (06 section 14.4 item 3; section 6 revision 6): Not robust.** The top of the matrix is A2 (305), which was not analysed; the recommended A4 configuration (300) is 5 points behind it and first in 4 of the 16 weight runs (C2 -10, C3 +10, C5 -10, C6 -10), A3 in 2 (C1 -10, C4 +10), A2 in 10; every single Low-cell move in A4's favour or against A2 (8 of 8) ties or reverses them. Between the recommended A4 and A5 the order holds: A4 first in all 16 weight runs (smallest lead 8.8 points, C1 -10), no single Low-cell move closes the 30 points, 19 of the 36 two-move combinations do. A4 scored with the JFET mixer (285) is not robust against A5 either (15 points; 3 weight runs and 6 of 9 single moves). Value of information (06 section 14.4 item 4): the analysis that would settle A2 is named in section 6 revision 6 item 4; the study does not run it, so A2 is presented to the owner with the finalists (Q1) under 06 section 14.5.
- **Pre-order checks (section 7.3, table R5-3):** closed as analyses: the thermal model with the Boyd drawing (gate item 7), the LPF, the transmit clock plan, the drive chain for A4; all six analysis reviews have now run (five reviewer APPROVED; the receiver's iteration 4 in progress). Run but not closed: the A5 drive window (select-on-test pad and a characterized level reading), REQ-SYS-012 (a requirement decision for both), the envelope loop (design items and the fault cases still to run in WP-PDR-22), the receiver image over temperature and MDS (2 + 3 + 4 and the front-end redesign in WP-PDR-19). Not yet done: the feed-resistance datasheet reads, the owner's ordering-gate reads, the in-situ sink and NTC-offset bench measurement (which now also sets the A4 tab cut-off margin), the Si5351A PLL relock time read (the frequency-check time budget), and the reviews of this revision 6 (INSP-110 iteration 3 re-issue 2, INSP-118 iteration 2). **Revision 8:** the receiver's iteration 4 has run (INSP-117, reviewer APPROVED, `123f048`), so all six analysis records are reviewer APPROVED, and the revision 6 reviews have run (section 10); the receiver image over temperature is closed as an analysis (75.37 dB), and its MDS stays open in WP-PDR-19.
- **Three findings for the owner before deciding:**
  1. **Neither finalist meets REQ-SYS-112 or REQ-SYS-012 as written.** Both need a duty-limited junction corner and a low-pack power delta; A4's is larger: its lowest corner at 6.4 V is 1.69 W, 3.7 dB under the 3.97 W floor, against 0.17 to 1.21 dB under it for A5 with the drain-feed and VGG levers at the filter median (1.13 to 2.51 dB at the filter worst case).
  2. **A5's worst case is over the USD 300 maximum** once the parts the analyses add are counted, and passes only through the guard ladder at the gate, by USD 0.17 after G1 and G2 (section 8.4).
  3. **The tinySA Ultra is on the critical path for going on the air, not for ordering.** The owner buys it later (status note 2026-09-28 section 1); the A4 harmonic assumption, A5's back-off harmonic ratio and the three spur lines over 25 uW close only at its sweep.
  4. **(Revision 6.) The TCXO is no longer optional for A4.** Without it REQ-SYS-182 and REQ-SYS-154 do not hold as written; with it both finalists meet them on the same counter route. It costs A4 USD 4.15 and its "no flagged part" advantage (C3), which is why A4 falls from 315 to 285 alone, and to 300 with the ring.
- **Owner's decision (section 10; revision 7):** **A5, decided 2026-09-29** (status note 2026-09-29 section 5, verbatim "A5"), after the owner's condition of section 4 ("I'm leaning towards A5 if it doesn't use outdated components") was answered by the A5 parts lifecycle check (`docs/research/a5-parts-lifecycle-2026-09-29.md`: 39 of 39 parts Active). This is not the recommended alternative (A4 with the TCXO and the ring, 300 against A5's 270); section 10 records the rationale, the conditions A5 carries and the design items fixed for it. The revision 6 text of Q1 (section 8.13) is kept as it was put to the owner.

## 2. Problem and decision context

- **Mission and system context:** the whole radio. The cost cap, the hand-assembly rule and the display removal touch every block of `docs/design/concept.md` sections 5 and 7, the L0 constraints CON-010, CON-015 and CON-026, NGO-027, NGO-028, MOE-007 and MOE-013, and about 80 L1 requirements (section 8.10).
- **Decision needed and intended outcome:** after the decision, the preliminary design has one architecture, one bill of materials with listed prices and sellers, one cost roll-up with an ordering gate, one user-interface concept (Morse audio menu) and one list of requirement deltas for a re-baseline CR. The PDR trades of WP-PDR-19 to 28 then close on this basis instead of the PCBWay turnkey basis.
- **Owner direction (verbatim extracts; the full statements are in the status note).** Section 6: "I think the first radio can't be more than $200 um, all in. Uh, you do not have to account for filament cost." "if I have to go ask a website for a quote for a part, that's a bad sign." "if we need to, you know, design our own amplifiers using MOSFETs, then so be it." "I've used JLCPCB before. So I'm down to get a bare PCB and then solder the through hole components myself." "I would be open to dropping the LCD screen and instead having a Morse code audio only interface." "you would press a button to enter the menu, and then you would hear the options in Morse code, and then you would pick like a letter to go to a different option, and then you can change numbers, and it will read it back to you to confirm that it was correct. And then you would say like Roger for like you know setting it, or um, N for no." Section 8: "Yeah, I'm flexible on the size. Cost is firm. [...] Um, the size and through hole are negotiable. I think ideally it's a through hole part, but if it needs to be a surface mount part, that's fine. I think the the uh, ones that get trickier are ball arrays. or other uh, chips that really require like flow soldering to do them accurately. But I can I can do surface mount parts." "I need to be able to solder it at my home bench you know and I don't have like a very sophisticated soldering setup. I have a heat gun um, I have solder I have flux". **Section 10 (governs the cost reading):** "I'm okay with, you know, I think the range is really $200 to $300, but $300 is really the absolute maximum. And yeah, I'm okay with not counting sales tax. I think I do have 24 gauge magnet wire. I have already a 2-meter antenna with an SMA male plug. Um, I have through-hole resistors and capacitors, but I don't have um, surface mount, and I do have a USB power adapter."
- **Lead SE reading applied here (status note section 10 for cost and owned stock; section 11 for cells, charger and instruments; section 8 items 2 to 6 for parts, board and UI; section 6 where both are silent):**
  1. Cost: target USD 200 for the first complete radio; USD 300 absolute maximum, and the worst case including 15 % contingency stays at or below it. Boards, parts, shipping, import duty and tariff pass-through count; sales tax and filament do not. Test instruments are outside the cap, under the owner's separate USD 300 cap on all new equipment (status note section 11).
  2. Owned (priced at USD 0): 24 AWG magnet wire, a 2 m antenna with an SMA-male plug, through-hole resistors and capacitors (values to be checked against the BOM), a USB power adapter, perfboard, two potentiometers, filament, headphones and a key or paddle. Not owned: surface-mount passives, 18650 cells and a charger (status note section 11: "I don't have any 18650s or chargers"; priced).
  3. Every part has a published price and stock at a catalog distributor or maker shop; nothing sold only on quote.
  4. Through-hole is preferred. Surface-mount parts are acceptable where the owner can hand-solder them with an iron, a heat gun, solder and flux (SOIC, SOT-23, SOT-89, SOT-223, SC-74A, TO-252, 1206 and 0805 passives, QFP and TSSOP of 0.65 mm pitch or more). Excluded: BGA and any part that in practice needs reflow or a stencil. Parts with a hidden exposed thermal pad, and leadless parts with pads under the body, are flagged. A pre-built module with pin headers or castellations and a listed price counts as through-hole compatible (to be confirmed).
  5. Discrete op-amp and MOSFET circuits, including a discrete PA, are welcome; a PA module with leads is a module.
  6. Board: a bare JLCPCB board soldered by the owner; perfboard for prototypes and non-RF sections.
  7. One radio first, in the owner-printed PETG case. Size is negotiable: REQ-SYS-102 (350 g) and REQ-SYS-103 (140 x 70 x 40 mm) are the ideal; deltas are reported.
  8. No display: the Morse-code audio menu replaces it.
  9. Risk per dollar (revision-2 brief, consistent with the section 10 reading that the charging, TCXO, surface-mount and low-pack-power trade-offs are re-evaluated under the ceiling): where the worst case still fits, the lower RF, thermal, emissions and build risk is preferred, and each upgrade's cost and the risk it retires are stated.
- **Constraints:** Part 97 (97.307(e): for a transmitter of 25 W or less each spurious emission is at most 25 uW and at least 40 dB down, which binds at 53.0 dBc at 5 W and 43.0 dBc at 0.5 W, REQ-SYS-017; 60 dBc design target, REQ-SYS-018). The owner's bench (SI-013 and status note section 11: NanoVNA with its calibration loads, a multimeter, a 50 ohm dummy load with a BNC connector, the SI-013 bench supply, a heat gun, solder and flux; no thermocouple; the tinySA Ultra of SI-034 not yet bought, to be bought later inside the USD 300 equipment cap (status note 2026-09-28 section 1); the multimeter is a Fluke 174 without a temperature input, so a K-type thermocouple thermometer is added to the equipment list (status note 2026-09-28 section 2)). The owner's rule that LTspice simulations of the amplifiers and filters must run and count as evidence (status note section 9); LTspice is accredited (ACC-LTSPICE-001, OD-24b, status note section 13).
- **Prior related decisions:** ADR-007 and SI-031 (PCBWay turnkey, owner through-hole only) and SI-028 (PA from DigiKey, Mouser or PCBWay distributors) are superseded if this study is adopted. TS-001 (PD54008L-E PA, Inrad or tolerance-designed ladder, SRR decision 54), TS-007 (Si5351A plus TCXO), TS-004 (4-layer 1.0 mm board), TS-011 (option C printed case with coating and CNC fallback; its M8 finding that PETG fails near a 4 W PA). CR-003 revision 3 and CR-006 revision 2 are Submitted and held for revision (status note section 6).
- **Research consulted (all 2026-09-25 to 2026-09-27):** the seven block research reports of this study (transmitter and PA, receiver, frequency generation, power and charging, UI and audio, fabrication and shipping, enclosure), quoted in section 11; `docs/research/pa-device-candidates.md` (F8 RA07M1317M, F10 AFT05MS004N, F16 to F20); `docs/research/cw-selectivity-options.md`; `docs/research/tr-switch-candidates.md`; `docs/research/keyer-and-key-interfaces.md` F10; the three architectures and three judge reports of section 4.3; the INSP-110 review record; the adversarial RF feasibility check of 2026-09-27 (22 claims, section 9); the author's own web reads marked "read today" in section 8.3.

## 3. Decision matrix setup and rationale

### 3.1 Criteria and operational definitions (revision 2)

Mandatory criteria are pass or fail; an alternative that fails one is dropped before scoring (SE HB section 6.8.1.2.1). Alternatives whose failure turns on an unverified line are still scored, for information and for the sensitivity run.

| ID | Criterion | Type | Operational definition | Scale | Weight |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | Cost ceiling | Mandatory | Worst case at or below USD 300: listed prices plus the high end of every estimated line, every shipping line at its high estimate, board duty at 92.5 % of the whole JLCPCB order (boards plus shipping; the base is unpublished), the Mouser tariff line at 50 % of the China-origin share, all times 1.15. Sales tax excluded (owner, section 10) | pass / fail | n/a |
| M2 | Listed prices | Mandatory | Every part has a published price and non-zero stock at a catalog distributor or maker shop on the read date (aggregator reads accepted provisionally, pending the owner's browser check); no part is sold only on quote | pass / fail | n/a |
| M3 | Hand-solderable | Mandatory | No BGA and no part that in practice needs reflow or a stencil. Leaded parts with pitch under 0.65 mm, leadless parts with pads under the body and hidden-pad parts are admitted only as flagged exceptions and are counted in C3 | pass / fail | n/a |
| M4 | Safety controls | Mandatory | Every hardware hazard control adopted at SRR (REQ-SYS-055, 083 to 085, 092, 120, 180, 181, 182) is implemented with parts used inside their published ratings, or its removal is recorded as a requirement delta for the owner; no Red safety risk that no step reduces to Yellow (06 section 13 item 2) | pass / fail | n/a |
| M5 | Emissions path | Mandatory | A design path to 97.307(e): a harmonic filter of at least 45 dB ideal attenuation at 2f plus a stated verification (LTspice and tinySA) | pass / fail | n/a |
| C1 | Expected cost against the target | Enhancing | Capped planning total (listed prices plus the midpoint of every estimated, shipping, duty and tariff line, times 1.15), USD. At or below the USD 200 target scores 5; above it, points are lost in proportion | 5: <= 200 / 3: 250 / 1: >= 300 (linear) | 20 |
| C2 | Worst-case margin | Enhancing | Capped worst case (the M1 figure), USD | 5: <= 220 / 3: 260 / 1: 300 (linear) | 5 |
| C3 | Hand-solderability | Enhancing | Count of flagged exceptions (hidden pad, leadless with pads under the body, pitch under 0.65 mm) | 1: three or more / 3: one / 5: none | 15 |
| C4 | Build and alignment effort | Enhancing | RF networks the owner designs or tunes by hand plus hand-wound filter, match or transformer coils, and board-area closure | 1: two or more hand matches, wound LPF and BPF, board does not close / 3: one hand match, wound BPF, catalog LPF / 5: no winding, no match to design | 10 |
| C5 | RF and emissions confidence | Enhancing | Evidence behind 5 W at the pack ends, the harmonic budget, stability and MDS: vendor harmonic and stability data, LPF construction, reference accuracy, mixer validation, T/R isolation | 1: no harmonic data, wound LPF, unvalidated mixer, power short of REQ-SYS-012 / 3: no PA harmonic data but catalog LPF and DPDT / 5: guaranteed harmonic and stability limits, catalog LPF, TCXO, validated mixer, DPDT, monitor port | 20 |
| C6 | Envelope and mass | Enhancing | Case volume against 140 x 70 x 40 mm and mass against 350 g | 1: over 20 % volume or over 370 g / 3: up to 8 % over, or one axis over / 5: inside on all axes and under 350 g | 10 |
| C7 | Requirement deltas and firmware scope | Enhancing | Number and size of requirement relaxations, unrecorded gaps and firmware added | 1: safety requirements dropped silently / 3: deltas recorded including KDR relaxations, moderate firmware / 5: wording-only deltas, no firmware added | 5 |
| C8 | Safety and thermal margin | Enhancing | Bounded PA junction against 110 C at 45 C ambient (REQ-SYS-112) at the worst corner (8.4 V pack, ALC back-off); heat path relative to cells and PETG; Catastrophic hazards kept in the box | 1: junction bound over 110 C / 3: bound under 110 C, or heat near PETG needing design controls, or an in-radio charger / 5: bound margin over 20 K, heat outside the case, no charger in the box. Where the junction figure is an estimate range, not a bound: a range wholly under 110 C reads as the bound anchor, a range that spans 110 C scores 2 and a range wholly over 110 C scores 1 (the rule A4 has been scored on since revision 2, stated in revision 4) | 15 |
| | | | | **Sum of weights** | **100** |

Criteria considered (06 section 13 item 1): safety used as M4 and C8; first power-on used as C4 (build and alignment effort is the main first-power-on driver); cost used as M1, C1 and C2; performance margin used as M5 and C5; schedule omitted because every alternative uses a JLCPCB bare board and catalog parts with similar lead times (about 1 to 2 weeks), so it does not discriminate; system security omitted because no alternative changes the USB firmware-loading or key-input attack surfaces of 07 section 16.2 (the Morse menu is common to all and its command path is assessed in WP-PDR-17).

Other criteria considered and not used: sourcing breadth (folded into M2, C5 and the risks of section 7.1), and the judges' three lenses as separate criteria (their content is carried by C1 and C2 for cost and sourcing; C5 and M5 for RF and regulatory; C3, C4, C6 and C8 for buildability, size and safety).

### 3.2 Alternatives

| ID | Alternative | Description | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| A0 | Current baseline (do nothing) | SRR concept: PCBWay 4-layer board with turnkey SMT, PD54008L-E PA, Inrad #111 or tolerance ladder with 24-bit ADC, LS013B7DH03 display, encoders, in-radio 2S charging, printed case with coating and a PCBWay CNC fallback; unit budget USD 610 over three units | `docs/plan/cost-estimate.md`; TS-001; TS-011; CR-003; CR-006 |
| A1 | min-cost (as submitted) | AFT05MS004NT1 SOT-89 PA with GVA-84+ driver, JFET superhet with hand-matched 500 Hz ladder, Morse-only UI, cells charged in an XTAR VC2, one Mouser order plus two JLCPCB boards | Architecture report "min-cost" |
| A2 | performance-in-cap PIC-5 | RD01MUS2 plus RD07MUS2B discrete pair (RF Parts), bare Si5351A with HCI TCXO, diode-ring superhet, ADC audio path, in-radio 2S charger (CN3302 chain, LCSC), four buttons | Architecture report "performance-in-cap" |
| A3 | buildability B1 | RA07M1317M module PA (RF Parts), no hand-wound parts (Coilcraft LPF, XRMW slug-tuned BPF from LCSC, TC1-1T+ mixer), bare Si5351A with TCXO, ADC audio, charging outside the radio, 40 dB monitor port, sink inside the case | Architecture report "buildability" |
| A4 | min-cost with grafts, revision 2 | Revision-1 A4 (A1 plus the Coilcraft LPF, G5V-2 DPDT relay, second LM393, plated slot and via field, XTAR MC1, ordering gate) repaired: spare AFT05 in the order (EOL, finding-1), the 74LVC1G80 divider chain (finding-3), 0.8 mm PA board and thermal compound (adversarial C4, C19), a drive low-pass filter, the monitor port in the baseline (finding-9), a second button (finding-7); owned wire and antenna; TCXO as an add-back. Revision 5 adds the common design items of section 8.14 (row E5) that the pre-order analyses require. **Revision 6 makes the TCXO a required item** (route R3 of REQ-SYS-182, D-17) and adds the REQ-SYS-181 sensor on the tab copper (D-6) and the PA_EN gating (D-18); the recommended configuration also takes A5's diode-ring mixer (U3) | This study, sections 7.3, 8.4 and 8.14 |
| A5 | hybrid H1 (presented with A4, section 8) | A4 revision 2 with the RA07M1317M module PA in place of the AFT05 and its hand match, the TG2520SMN TCXO fitted, the diode-ring mixer on two BN-43-202 cores wound from the owner's 24 AWG wire in place of the JFET mixer, and a spare GVA-84+; revision 3 adds a VGG clamp and a vented PA bay; revision 4 sizes the clamp at 3.46 V or less over the LDO's temperature range and 1 % resistors (no cost); revision 5 adds the common design items of section 8.14 (row E5) and the A5-only select-on-test drive pad; revision 6 adds the route R3 counter input and the PA_EN gating (D-17, D-18) | This study, sections 8.1 to 8.4 and 8.14 |

Alternatives pruned before scoring, with reason:
- **All-through-hole radio.** No through-hole VHF final is in active production at a catalog distributor; the NOS RD06HVF1 (TO-220, RF Parts USD 9.91) gives an estimated 3.5 to 4.5 W at 7.2 V and about 2.8 W at 6.0 V, and TO-39 bipolar chains give 0.5 to 1 W (transmitter research). Pruned as infeasible at 5 W.
- **Generic MOSFET PA (IRF510, BS170, 2N7000).** 0 to 5 dB of power gain at 144 MHz for the IRF510 (Ciss 135 to 180 pF, 7 nH source lead); the BS170 and 2N7000 are 0.1 to 0.3 W pre-drivers at best (estimate). Pruned on physics.
- **Direct-conversion receiver.** 0 dB opposite-sideband response and a 3 dB double-sideband noise penalty; fails REQ-SYS-022 and 025. Kept only as a perfboard audio-chain prototype.
- **SA612 mixer, commercial CW filters (Inrad #111 USD 118, KVG on quote), LMX2571 synthesizer (WQFN with exposed pad, 0.5 mm).** Discontinued, over budget for their function, sold on quote, or reflow-only.
- **In-radio 2S charging (revision 2 re-check under the ceiling).** The USB-input 2S charger ICs found (CN3302 with HY2213 balancers and FS8205A switches) are LCSC-only; no leaded USB-to-2S charger IC with a Mouser listing was found in the power research. Adding it costs an estimated USD 7 to 14 capped in parts plus USD 0 to 23 capped of LCSC shipping if the JLCPCB merge is not offered, and puts the Catastrophic HZ-002 charger chain back in the box beside a PA sink that dissipates 8.5 to 10 W. Not cheap and not safer; pruned for build 1 (owner question Q3, section 8.13).
- **A spare RA07M1317M in the baseline.** USD 33.25 capped; it pushes the A5 worst case to about USD 327.8, over the ceiling. Kept as a gate add-back (AB-A, section 8.4).
- **Perfboard or Manhattan for the RF sections.** Admitted for prototypes and non-RF sections only; a 144 MHz LPF, drive chain and front end need controlled ground and short returns that a 2-layer board with a solid pour gives for USD 2.

### 3.3 Weight rationale

Cost carries 25 (C1 20, C2 5), down from 35 in revision 1, because the owner's section 10 answer turns the firm USD 200 cap into a USD 200 target with a USD 300 absolute maximum: the ceiling is enforced by M1, and C1 now scores the distance above the target. The 10 points freed go to RF and emissions confidence (C5, 15 to 20; a legal limit, 97.307(e), and the radio's purpose) and to safety and thermal margin (C8, 10 to 15; REQ-SYS-112 is a KDR and the PETG case has a 69 C heat-deflection temperature, TS-011 M8), following the risk-per-dollar reading of section 2 item 9. Hand-solderability (C3, 15) stays high because it is the owner's stated reason for the SMD boundary. Build effort (C4, 10) and envelope (C6, 10, negotiable) follow; deltas and firmware scope (C7, 5) matter least because the Morse UI adds the same firmware to every alternative. No weight was set by the owner directly.

## 4. Scoring rationale

### 4.1 Cost re-roll under the owner budget and mandatory screening

**Re-roll method.** Every alternative is re-rolled with the section 10 budget: owned items removed (magnet wire, the SMA-male antenna plug, through-hole resistors and capacitors), no sales tax, and the same shipping, duty and tariff treatment: board duty 35 % to 92.5 % (JLCPCB US tariff FAQ, base unpublished; planning on the board value, worst case on boards plus shipping), Mouser tariff pass-through USD 4 to 12 at planning and USD 15 worst (50 % of a USD 30 China-origin share, the Section 301 rate on HTS 8541 and 8542 reported for 2025), 15 % contingency on every line. A4 and A5 are re-rolled line by line (section 8.3). A1, A2 and A3 are re-rolled from their architects' totals, because their line items were not re-read: the owner credits are the architects' own guard values where they gave one (A2's magnet-wire guard G1 -14.28), otherwise the A4 values (antenna plug -5.18 planning and -6.90 high, through-hole passives -4.03 and -1.73); those three rows are **Low** confidence.

**Revision 5: row E5 on every alternative.** The pre-order analyses add parts that every alternative with this receiver, drive chain, keying loop and clock plan needs (section 8.14): a third receiver BPF section and J310 port matching, the keying-loop items, the CLK1 coax interface, supply filters, a PA-case NTC, and the recommended drive bandpass and 2 % LPF parts. Row E5 carries them as an estimate of USD 5.19 to 17.63 before contingency (planning at the midpoint, 11.41), that is USD 5.96 / 13.12 / 20.27 capped, added to every row below. The spur analysis said its own items fit inside the existing E rows and contingency; this study does not take that, because the E2 row covers about 22 values already counted and the gate contingency covers build-time growth, not design additions (section 8.4). Figures in brackets are revision 4.

**Revision 6: item (g) of row E5 and the TCXO in A4.** Row E5 gains item (g), the PA_EN gate, its clamp FET, the TCXO squaring buffer for the second GPIN and a GVA-84+ supply P-FET where a line-up lacks one (USD 0.30 to 1.50 before contingency, estimate; section 8.14 D-17 and D-18). E5 is now USD 5.49 to 19.13 before contingency (planning 12.31), USD 6.31 / 14.15 / 21.99 capped, on every row below. A4 also carries the TG2520SMN (Mouser USD 3.61 listed, 4.15 capped) as a required item. Figures in brackets are revision 5.

**Revision 5 re-scoring (on the analysis evidence; section 7.3 table R5-2).** Ten cells change. C1: A5 4 to 3 (row E5). C2: A2 and A4 4 to 3 (row E5). C5: A5 5 to 4 and A3 5 to 4 (the module findings). C6: A4 3 to 1 and A5 2 to 1 (the wrap-around guard REQ-SYS-113 needs). C8: A5 2 to 1 and A3 2 to 1 (junction range wholly over 110 C), A2 3 to 2 (evidence parity, stated in its row). Unchanged on the evidence: C3 (no new flagged part: the added 74LVC1G66 and 2N7002 class parts are SC-74A and SOT-23), C4 (the third BPF section and the per-resonator alignment apply to every alternative with this receiver; A5's select-on-test pad is a tuning step that moves A5 toward 3, carried as a note, not a score), C7 (both finalists add a REQ-SYS-112 delta, a REQ-SYS-118 setpoint and loop firmware; A4 also a wider REQ-SYS-012 delta and a REQ-TX-014 change, A5 a firm REQ-SYS-012 delta and the REQ-SYS-144 pad step: both stay at "deltas recorded including KDR relaxations, moderate firmware"). M4 still passes for both, with one note for A5: open loop at 8.4 V from a -10 C start the module may exceed its 10 W maximum rating from 8.1 V (estimate, PA drive analysis section 5 (g)), so the pack-dependent VGG clamp that revision 4 held as a fallback becomes a design item (section 8.14, no cost), with REQ-SYS-156 and the 10 s cutoff as the time bounds. A2 and A3 were not finalists and were not analysed; only the common-basis items (E5 costs), the items that follow from sharing A5's module (A3) and the evidence-parity reading of A2's junction figure are applied to them.

**Revision 6 re-scoring (INSP-118 findings 1 to 3; section 7.3).** One cell changes on the evidence: **C3-A4 5 to 3**, because the reviewed frequency-budget method shows that A4 holds REQ-SYS-182 and REQ-SYS-154 as written only with a countable TCXO (route R3), so the TG2520SMN, the one flagged part, is now required. Every other cell holds: the TCXO and the item (g) parts move C1-A4 from 4.97 to 4.76 and C2-A4 from 2.8 to 2.52 (both still round to 5 and 3); C5-A4 stays 2 (the 30 ppm term goes, the power, harmonic-data and mixer terms stay); C7-A4 stays 3; C8 is set by the REQ-SYS-112 corner, which the tab sensor does not change; item (g) moves no other alternative's cell (A2 C1 5.0, C2 3.2; A3 C1 2.85, C2 1; A5 C1 3.05, C2 1). The recommended configuration adds U3: C5-A4 reads 3 with the TCXO and the ring together (revision 5's hybrid reading) and C2-A4 falls to 2 (272.17). A2's C6 is not re-scored by parity with the finalists' wrap guard, although INSP-110 finding-19 asked why only C8 was: C8-A2's parity reading rests on a model finding that applies to any catalog-rated sink in a closed case (in-situ 5.6 to 7.9 K/W against the 2.6 K/W rating), while the finalists' C6 loss comes from their own sink-end layout (the sink wholly outside the end wall and a wrap-around guard for REQ-SYS-113), which A2's architect did not draw and no record has modelled. Section 6 revision 6 item 4 carries the C6 reading as a sensitivity (A2 275 to 295).

### 4.3 Independent architects' and judges' results (inputs to this study)

Three architects worked independently, one per angle (minimum cost, performance inside the cap, buildability); three judges then scored all three, each through a different lens. Their numbers were produced under the revision-1 cap of USD 200 and are reproduced here unchanged as evidence; this study's own matrix is section 5.

**Architect totals (capped, including 15 % contingency, as submitted):**

| Architecture | Low | Planning or expected | High |
|---|---|---|---|
| min-cost (A1) | 169.15 | (midpoint 184.16) | 199.17 |
| PIC-5 (A2) | 165.50 | 196.92 | 218.28 |
| B1 (A3) | 183.05 | 199.23 | 240.02 |

**Judge 0 (cost-and-sourcing lens; 1 to 10; weighted = cost x3, sourcing x2, envelope x2, others x1, maximum 120):**

| Criterion | min-cost | PIC-5 | B1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fits USD 200 with contingency | 8 | 6 | 5 |
| Listed-price sourcing verified | 5 | 6 | 6 |
| Envelope and mass | 5 | 7 | 7 |
| Hand-solderability | 9 | 4 | 6 |
| 97.307(e) and RF credibility | 5 | 6 | 8 |
| Safety controls kept | 4 | 7 | 7 |
| Owner buildability | 6 | 4 | 8 |
| Firmware scope | 7 | 5 | 6 |
| Requirement deltas | 4 | 7 | 6 |
| **Unweighted (/90)** | **53** | **52** | **59** |
| **Weighted (/120)** | **79** | **77** | **82** |

Judge 0's ranking put min-cost first, "ONLY IF" REQ-SYS-055 and REQ-SYS-182 are restored, although B1 has the higher weighted total: the judge ranked on the cost cap, not on the total. Revision 1 resolved that by making the cap mandatory; revision 2 keeps the ceiling mandatory (M1) and scores the target distance (C1).

**Judge 1 (RF-and-regulatory lens; 1 to 10; unweighted sum):** min-cost 60, B1 57, PIC-5 51. Cost: 8 / 4 / 5 (min-cost / B1 / PIC-5); sourcing 7 / 6 / 6; envelope 6 / 6 / 8; hand-solderable 9 / 6 / 4; emissions and RF 5 / 9 / 8; safety 6 / 6 / 7; buildability 5 / 8 / 4; firmware 8 / 5 / 4; deltas 6 / 7 / 5. Ranking: min-cost, B1, PIC-5.

**Judge 2 (owner buildability, size and safety lens; 1 to 10; weighted out of 10):** min-cost 6.8, PIC-5 5.9, B1 5.6. Cost 8 / 5 / 4 (min-cost / PIC-5 / B1); sourcing 6 / 6 / 6; envelope 6 / 8 / 6; hand-solder 9 / 4 / 6; emissions 6 / 7 / 8; safety 6 / 7 / 5; buildability 7 / 4 / 7; firmware 8 / 5 / 5; deltas 4 / 6 / 6. Ranking: min-cost, PIC-5, B1.

**All three judges ranked min-cost first, each conditionally, under the USD 200 cap.** Their arithmetic checks reproduced the architects' totals, with these corrections: min-cost's own AO3400A flag (+0.86) moves its high case to 200.16 (judge 1); PIC-5's planning total is 196.93, not 196.92 (rounding); B1's itemized expected rows re-sum to about USD 199.63 (judge 2). Judge 1 also found B1 the strongest RF design (9 of 10), and every judge's cost preference for min-cost was a preference under a cap that the owner has since moved.

**Findings the judges called fatal or near-fatal, and their treatment in revision 2:**

| Finding (judge) | Treatment |
|---|---|
| min-cost drops REQ-SYS-055 and REQ-SYS-182 with no delta (0, 2) | Restored in A4 and A5: second LM393P monostable; 74LVC1G80GV divide-by-8 chain into an RP2350 PWM edge counter (section 8.1); revision 6: into the RP2350 FC0 counter on GPIN0, with the TCXO on GPIN1 (route R3, section 7.3) |
| min-cost PA thermal path: 8 vias of 0.3 mm give Tj about 160 C (1) | A4: plated slot, via field, 0.8 mm board, compound; still not shown under 110 C. A5: module flange on the sink through compound, bound 103 C (section 7.3) |
| No architecture counts Mouser's tariff pass-through (2) | Carried in every total, worst case at 50 % |
| min-cost has no AFT05 harmonic data and wound LPF coils; SPDT relay overstresses the RX clamps (0, 1, 2) | Coilcraft LPF and G5V-2 DPDT in A4 and A5; A5's module has guaranteed harmonic maxima |
| Sourcing evidence for Mouser is aggregator-only (0) | Owner price-check list (section 8.11) before ordering |
| Boyd sink height disputed (0, 1, 2) | Settled at 25.4 mm fin depth and 41.9 mm height (Farnell and Newark listings); the double-centre-channel profile needs the drawing check of section 8.11 |
| PIC-5 and B1 high cases break the USD 200 cap; PIC-5 board over-full; B1 sink inside PETG beside the cells (0, 1, 2) | Cap moved to USD 300 worst; scored in C2, C4, C8 |

Revision 6: the INSP-118 conditions move A4 by -30 (C3, the required TCXO) and, with the ring, the recommended configuration by -15 from its revision 5 total (C3 -30, C5 +20, C2 -5). It leads A5 by 30 points, over the 25-point rule of 06 section 14.5; A4 with the JFET mixer leads A5 by 15, under it. A2, 305, is 5 points above the recommended configuration and 20 above A4 with the JFET mixer; it was not a finalist and was not analysed, and it is presented to the owner with them (section 6 revision 6 item 4; 06 section 14.5). Revision 5: A4 315, A2 305, A3 285, A5 270; the pre-order analyses had moved A5 by -65 (C1 -20 on the E5 cost, C5 -20 on the low-pack power and drive window, C6 -10 on the guard envelope, C8 -15 on a junction range wholly over 110 C) and A4 by -25 (C2 -5, C6 -20). Revision 4 had A4 340 and A5 335, presented together.

## 6. Uncertainty and sensitivity statement

**Revision 6 (governs; items 1 to 8 below are revision 5 and stay for the review trail).** The revision 6 script (author's scratchpad, not committed; the TS is committed alone) first reproduces every revision 5 figure of this section: totals A4 315, A2 305, A3 285, A5 270; A4 first in 12 of 16 weight runs and A2 in 4; A4's smallest lead over A5 14.4 (C5 +10) and largest 75.6 (C5 -10); 46 of 84 three-move combinations; the five A2 single moves; the joint adverse cases 365 / 345 / 335 / 230 and 345 / 340 / 335 / 255. It then runs revision 6.

- **R6-1. Weight sensitivity** (every weight +10 and -10, the others rescaled to keep 100; 16 runs, A2 to A5).
  - With the recommended A4 + U3 (300): **A2 first in 10 runs, A4 + U3 in 4** (C2 -10: 310.5 against A2 305.5; C3 +10: 300.0 against 280.9; C5 -10: 300.0 against 293.1; C6 -10: 322.2 against 294.4), **A3 in 2** (C1 -10: A3 283.1, A2 280.6, A4 + U3 275.0; C4 +10: A3 308.9, A4 + U3 300.0). A5 in none. Between A4 + U3 and A5 alone, A4 + U3 is first in all 16; its smallest lead is 8.8 points (C1 -10: 275.0 against 266.2), its largest 51.2 (C1 +10).
  - With A4 alone (285, JFET mixer): A2 first in 11, A4 in 3 (C3 +10, C5 -10, C6 -10), A3 in 2. Between A4 and A5, A4 is first in 13; A5 leads at C1 -10 (266.2 against 258.1), C2 -10 (287.9 against 283.4) and C5 +10 (286.2 against 274.4).
- **R6-2. Score sensitivity** (every Low cell +1 and -1; Low cells as in revision 5: C1, C2 and C8 for all; C5 for A3, A4 and A5; C6 for A2, A4 and A5).
  - **A2 against A4 + U3 (5 points):** every one of the 8 single moves in A4 + U3's favour (A2 C1, C2, C6 or C8 -1; A4 + U3 C2, C5, C6 or C8 +1) ties or reverses them (A4 + U3 C5 +1: 320 against 305). Seven single moves the other way widen A2's lead (to 320 against 300 at A2 C8 +1).
  - **A4 + U3 against A5 (30 points):** no single move closes it; 19 of the 36 two-move combinations and 81 of the 84 three-move combinations reach a tie or better for A5 (for example A5 C1 +1 and A4 + U3 C5 -1: 290 against 280).
  - **A4 against A5 (15 points):** 6 of the 9 single moves reach a tie or better for A5 (A5 C1 or C5 +1: 290 against 285; A4 C1 or C5 -1: 265 against 270).
  - **Joint adverse cases** (every Low cell of the target down one, every other Low cell up one; with A4 + U3): against A5, A4 + U3 350, A3 345, A2 335, A5 230; against A4 + U3, A3 345, A5 340, A2 335, A4 + U3 240; against A2, A4 + U3 350, A3 345, A5 340, A2 255.
- **R6-3. Named readings (not scored).** Duty-limited C8 for A5 (2): A5 285, still 15 under A4 + U3. A2 with the finalists' wrap-guard envelope (C6 -1 to -3): A2 295, 285 or 275, below A4 + U3 in every case. Needed-only E5 (without the optional drive bandpass and 2 % LPF parts): A5 243.78 planning and 309.38 worst (still over USD 300); A4 200.86 and 262.44; A4 + U3 203.46 and 265.04; no score changes.
- **R6-4. A2 (06 section 14.4 item 4 and section 14.5; INSP-110 finding-19).** A2 is the top-ranked alternative on the matrix and was never analysed. The study does not recommend it, and 06 section 14.5 requires the report to say why and to present it with the finalists:
  - **Why its total is not taken at face value:** four of its eight cells are Low-confidence estimates from its architect (C1, C2, C6, C8), and the analyses moved exactly those kinds of cells down for both analysed finalists (C6 by 1 to 2 points, C8 by 1 for A5, C1 and C2 by the E5 parts). Its C8 already takes the parity reading (section 4.2); its C6 does not (section 4.2, revision 6 paragraph), and with the finalists' guard envelope it would total 275 to 295.
  - **Why it is not recommended even at its total:** three flagged parts (the hidden-pad leadless RD07MUS2B, the 0.5 mm MSOP Si5351A and the leadless TCXO); a new-old-stock PA pair from RF Parts, the RD07MUS2B "no longer available for export" (supply risk: a blown final may have no replacement); a CN3302 charger chain sold only by LCSC, whose merge into the JLCPCB parcel is unverified; and the Catastrophic HZ-002 charger chain in the box beside a PA that dissipates in A4's range.
  - **What it offers that neither finalist does:** in-radio USB charging (SI-022, CON-010, given up by both finalists, D2), and the smallest envelope as drawn.
  - **The analysis that would settle it (value of information):** the WP-PDR-28 thermal model re-run on A2's layout (RD07MUS2B pair on its sink, charger in the case; C6 and C8), the WP-PDR-21 drive and output-power runs for the RD01MUS2 and RD07MUS2B line-up at 6.4 V (C5), and the ordering-gate reads of the RF Parts stock and the LCSC merge (M2, C1, C2). Cost: about two working days of analysis plus their reviews, no money; gate: before the owner's Q1 decision if the owner wants A2 in the choice set. The study recommends not running it, for the supply and HZ-002 reasons above, and asks the owner's concurrence that the choice set is A4 and A5 (Q1).
- **R6-5. Robustness verdict (06 section 14.4 item 3): Not robust.** The top rank (A2) changes under 6 of the 16 weight runs and under every single Low-cell move in the recommended A4's favour. Between the finalists the order holds on weights (A4 + U3 first in 16 of 16) and against any single Low-cell move (30 points, over the 25-point rule); two moves can close it. A4 without the ring is not robust against A5 (15 points; 3 weight runs, 6 single moves). The recommendation (section 8) is therefore A4 + U3, presented together with A2 and with A5 (06 section 14.5).
- **R6-6. Evidence asymmetry and the review state.** All six analysis reviews have now run (table R5-1): five are reviewer APPROVED and none changed a figure this study scores on (INSP-114 X-8, INSP-116 X-8). The receiver record's iteration 4 (2 + 3 + 4 over temperature) is in progress (**revision 8, INSP-110 finding-26:** it has since run, reviewer APPROVED at `123f048`, 75.37 dB worst case over temperature; all six records are now APPROVED); the filter change is inside row E5 (a) and moves the MDS of both finalists in the same direction, so it moves no cell (section 7.3). The three INSP-118 conditions are no-cost or low-cost design items (D-6, D-17, D-18) plus the TCXO; the A4 tab cut-off margin (about 8 K at the all-adverse corner) rests on the lumped thermal model and the bench NTC-offset measurement (section 7.3, revision 6).

The revision 5 runs use the same script as revision 4 (author's scratchpad, not committed), which first reproduces every revision 4 figure of this section (A4 first in 9 of 16 weight runs, A5 in 5, A2 in 2; the ten Low-cell moves; the joint adverse cases 380 / 290 and 385 / 280) before the revision 5 matrix is run.

1. **Weight sensitivity (revision 5).** Every weight moved by +10 and -10 points, the others rescaled to keep 100 (16 runs, A2 to A5). **A4 is first in 12 runs; A2 in 4** (C3 at -10: A2 329.1, A3 295.0; C4 at -10: A2 327.8, A4 316.7; C5 at +10: A2 316.9, A4 300.6; C6 at +10: A2 315.6, A4 291.1). **A5 is first in none.** Between the two finalists alone, A4 is first in all 16; A4's smallest lead over A5 is 14.4 points (C5 at +10: A4 300.6, A5 286.2), its largest 75.6 (C5 at -10). Revision 4 had A4 first in 9, A5 in 5, A2 in 2.
2. **Score sensitivity.** Every Low-confidence cell moved by +1 and -1 one at a time: C1, C2 and C8 for all; C5 for A3, A4 and A5 (A5 and A3 become Low in revision 5, because their C5 now rests on graph reads, a back-off ratio estimate and an estimated PA temperature coefficient); C6 for A2, A4 and A5 (A4 becomes Low: no case CAD). **No single move changes the order of A4 and A5.** The largest single moves for A5 (C1 or C5 +1) give 290 against A4's 315; A5 C8 +1 (the duty-limited reading below) gives 285. **Two moves do not close it either; three do**, for example A5 C1 +1, A5 C5 +1 and A4 C5 -1 (A5 310, A4 295), or A5 C1, C2 and C5 +1 (a 315 tie); 46 of the three-move combinations reach a tie or better for A5. Five single moves put A2 level with or ahead of A4: A2 C6 +1 (tie at 315), A2 C8 +1 (A2 320), A4 C1 -1, A4 C5 -1 (A2 305, A4 295) and A4 C8 -1 (A2 305, A4 300). The joint adverse case (every Low cell of the target down one, every other Low cell up one; beyond the 14.4 rule): against A5, A4 365, A3 345, A2 335, A5 230; against A4, A3 345, A5 340, A2 335, A4 255.
3. **Named readings that the owner may prefer (not scored):**
   - **Duty-limited junction corner.** Both finalists need the REQ-SYS-112 delta to a duty-limited corner (section 8.10). Scored at that corner (50 % duty, 13 s key-downs, 45 C), both junction figures are OPEN ranges that span 110 C (A4 105.6 C +16.3 K, A5 104.4 C +12.4 K), so C8 would be 2 for both: A5 285, A4 315. A5's box still has two failures outside their bands (V08, V17), so 2 is the ceiling.
   - **Needed-only E5.** Without the optional drive bandpass and the 2 % LPF parts, E5 is USD 2.60 to 11.43 before contingency: A5 plans at 242.74 (C1 3.3, still 3) and its worst case is 307.65 (still over USD 300). A4 plans at 195.67 and its worst is 256.56 (C2 3.2, still 3). No score changes.
   - **Revision 4 envelope (no wrap guard).** If a lighter guard were shown to hold REQ-SYS-113 for A4 (its fin tips were 49.7 C, 1.7 K over, as revision 4 drew them), A4's C6 could return to 3 (A4 335). No such relief exists for A5 (64.2 C, 16.2 K over).
   - **Hybrid A4 with the diode ring (U3).** 315, equal to A4 (C4 stays 3; C5 stays 2 because the harmonic-data and low-pack power terms dominate), for USD 2.60 capped (planning 203.33, worst 266.29). **Hybrid A4 with the ring and the TCXO (U2 and U3):** 300 (C3 5 to 3 for the flagged TCXO outweighs C5 2 to 3). Neither beats A4 on the matrix; the ring is recommended as an option on evidence quality (section 8).
4. **Evidence asymmetry against A2 and A3.** The six analyses ran for A4 and A5 only, on the owner's approval of 2026-09-28. A2's C6 (4) and C5 (4) cells, and A3's C6 (3), are still the architects' estimates. The thermal model moved both analysed finalists down on exactly those kinds of cells (C6 by 1 to 2 points, C8 by 1 for A5). A2's C8 is already read at 2 on evidence parity (section 4.2). If A2's envelope took the same wrap-around guard (its PA dissipates in the same range as A4's), its C6 would fall by 1 to 3 points and A2 would total 275 to 295, below A4. A2 also has three flagged parts, a new-old-stock PA "no longer available for export" and an LCSC-only charger chain, so it is not proposed as a finalist; the owner question stays between A4 and A5.
5. **Assumptions and their evidence:**
   - Mouser prices are the aggregator republication of Mouser's own price and stock (section 8.3). Rows read today: 74LVC1G80GV, TG2520SMN, CONSMA001-C-G, Wakefield 120-SA, Fair-Rite 2843000202 and 2843002402, KEMET C0805C101J5GACTU, TC1-1T+, 529802B02500G and 530002B02500G; INSP-110 re-read 25 more rows the same day.
   - RA07M1317M-501: RF Parts USD 28.91, "In Stock", "New", no end-of-life note, USD 15 minimum order (read 2026-09-27); Mitsubishi Electric US "Active". Datasheet values (June 2019) are those recorded in `docs/research/pa-device-candidates.md` F8 and read again by the thermal and PA drive analyses on 2026-09-28.
   - RF Parts shipping is "calculated based on weight / dimensions vs destination" with a USD 2.75 residential UPS surcharge (search summary); carried as USD 10 to 18 (E).
   - Mouser free shipping starts at USD 100 in the US (search summary; Low). A5's Mouser merchandise is about USD 96 to 123 with row E5 (revision 4: 90.89 to 105.39), so the threshold is more likely crossed; the worst case still carries USD 8 of shipping.
   - JLCPCB US tariff FAQ: "Total tariff rate: About 35%~92.5%", base not published. JLCPCB shipping to the US USD 12 to 25.
   - Row E5 (revision 5) is an estimate built from the section 8.3 row prices and the E2 per-value range (section 8.14); the CLK1 coax interface is not read (the Adafruit edge-launch SMA, product 1865, lists USD 2.50 and is out of stock, read 2026-09-28; not carried, because the coax is soldered).
   - Every performance and temperature figure from the analyses is an estimate: PA curves are graph reads of typical data, the thermal model is a lumped network with class-E derates, the spur levels are Low-confidence estimates with 20 to 50 dB ranges, and the keying budget terms are estimates (section 7.3 table R5-2, confidence column).
6. **Robustness verdict:** **Robust between the finalists** on weights (A4 first in 16 of 16) and on scores (three simultaneous Low-cell moves needed; 45-point lead against the 25-point rule). **Not robust against A2** (10 points, and four weight runs and five single moves put A2 level or first), but A2 carries unanalysed cells in the direction the analyses moved every analysed cell, and is not a finalist (item 4). **Cost screen:** A5 does not pass M1 on its stacked worst case (USD 314.78) and passes only through guards G1 and G2 (298.10, margin USD 1.90) at the gate; A4 passes by USD 36.31.
7. **Value of information.** Before the order: the owner's browser reads of section 8.11 (they settle the cost screen, now decisive for A5), the DMP3099L and MF-R300 datasheet reads (feed resistance, both finalists), and, for A4, the WP-PDR-21 match run (PA drive analysis C5), which replaces the (Vd/7.5)^n estimate and the assumed drain efficiency that set A4's power and its junction band (+15 K from the AFT05 current alone). The in-situ sink measurement (a power resistor on the actual Boyd part inside a printed guard coupon; thermal note section 9.1 item 2) replaces the orientation derate, the largest thermal input, for both. After the order and before first on-air use: the tinySA sweep (A4 harmonics, A5 back-off ratio, the three spur lines) and the keying calibration measurements (keying note section 8 item 7), which decide A5's first-element result. None of these is expected to reverse the A4-over-A5 order: the matrix needs three one-point moves in A5's favour.
8. **Limitations of the evaluation methods and tools:** prices are single-day reads, mostly through an aggregator; shipping, tariff and duty lines and row E5 are estimates; no cart was opened. A1 to A3 re-rolls use their architects' totals plus E5. The analyses are developer evidence (their Python checkers have no TV record) with LTspice accredited (ACC-LTSPICE-001 for the `tools/ltspice-batch.sh` wrapper blob 88b71475, LTspice 26.0.2). Three of the six records are still under review (section 9 table R5-1): the PA drive and keying notes answered Major findings in revisions not yet re-reviewed, and the receiver BPF note's revision 3 is not yet re-reviewed. If a re-review changes a figure this study uses, section 10 names it as a revisit condition. The thermal model has no bench correlation. The judges' scores are expert judgement.

### 7.2 Benefits beyond the scored criteria

| Alt | Benefits |
|---|---|
| A5 | The PA is a 50 ohm block with a vendor datasheet that guarantees output, efficiency, harmonics, load ruggedness (20:1 at 9.2 V and 7 W) and stability (spurious below -60 dBc for VDD 4.0 to 9.2 V, Pin 10 to 30 mW, Pout up to 8 W, load VSWR 4:1), and it is in current production ("Active"). "This module is designed for manual soldering" (datasheet). (Revision 5: the stability guarantee holds only with the module drive inside 10 to 30 mW, which the fixed pad does not keep, and the harmonic guarantee does not cover the 5 W, 8.4 V back-off state; section 7.3.) The TCXO also shrinks the REQ-SYS-182 error budget from 8.9 to 4.8 kHz (revision 6: withdrawn; on the reviewed method the TCXO makes route R3 possible, which A4 now also takes, section 7.3). The diode ring is the receiver research's own mixer. Charging outside the radio keeps the Catastrophic HZ-002 charger chain out of the box. Analog audio keeps the TS-001 audio decision. |
| A4 | Cheapest compliant radio (USD 187.61 planning in revision 4; revision 6 USD 205.91, 208.51 with the ring); one distributor plus the boards and cells; no leadless part until revision 6 (the required TCXO is the one). The AFT05 is rated for 65:1 VSWR at 9 V. Its 136 to 941 MHz range is not claimed as a later 70 cm benefit, because the device is End of Life. |
| A2 | Keeps in-radio charging, the TCXO and the best envelope. |
| A3 | Easiest build: no winding, no PA match; guaranteed harmonics. |
