**Revision 6: frequency verification, REQ-SYS-182 and REQ-SYS-154 (INSP-118 finding-1).** Revision 5's budget ("2.5 ppm (TCXO) + 30 ppm (Pico 2 crystal, recalled) ... 5.0 kHz inside 10 kHz; A4 9.0 kHz") is withdrawn. It used a recalled crystal value, left out the counter's own accuracy and ignored the rule that a measured threshold must sit between the healthy disagreement and the true-error limit. This revision adopts the method of the reviewed `docs/design/analysis/frequency-budget.md` section 3.3 (WP-PDR-20; INSP-056 APPROVED) and its checker `hardware/sim/freq/freq_budget.py`, re-run for this revision:
- **Counter.** The RP2350 frequency counter FC0, not a PWM edge counter, counts the /8 prescaler output (18.0 to 18.5 MHz) on GPIN0, timed by clk_ref from the Pico 2 crystal (XOSC), independent of the synthesizer and its driver; FC0 sets DIED when the counted clock stops ("no valid measurement means no agreement"). Interval 12 (4 ms, accuracy 500 Hz at the input, 4 kHz at the carrier; RP2350 datasheet Table 541) before PA_EN, interval 13 (8 ms, 2 kHz at the carrier) repeated during transmission.
- **Timebase.** The Pico 2 crystal is 65 ppm (30 tolerance + 30 stability + 5 first-year aging, RP2350 datasheet Table 596), not 30 ppm: 9.62 kHz at 147.9988 MHz.
- **Threshold rule.** A measured threshold T meets REQ-SYS-154 (true error over 10 kHz) only if the healthy disagreement d stays below T (no false trip) and T + d is at most 10 kHz (no missed trip), so d must stay under 5 kHz.
- **Route R1 (raw XOSC timebase):** d = 4.0 kHz (counter) + reference error + 9.62 kHz (XOSC). With a 2.5 ppm TCXO d is 13.99 kHz at interval 12; with A4's 30 ppm module crystal it is 18.06 kHz at interval 12 and 16.06 kHz at interval 13. No threshold meets REQ-SYS-154 at 10 kHz; R1 would need, by CR, REQ-SYS-182 widened to at least 16.1 kHz and REQ-SYS-154 to at least 27.7 kHz (interval 13) for A4 without a TCXO, and to 12.0 and 23.7 kHz with one.
- **Route R3 (adopted, both finalists):** FC0 counts the TCXO on GPIN1 during receive (interval 15) and refreshes the XOSC/TCXO ratio at least once a second, accepted only within +/-67.5 ppm; in a healthy unit carrier and TCXO are in an exact ratio, so the reference error cancels. d = 4.0 kHz + 370 Hz (TCXO count quantization, 62.5 Hz in 25 MHz) + 148 Hz (1 ppm XOSC drift allocation) = **4 518 Hz at interval 12** and 2 518 Hz at interval 13. With **T = 5.0 kHz**: no false trip with 482 Hz of margin at interval 12 (2 482 Hz at 13); the largest undetected true error is 9 518 Hz, so REQ-SYS-154 and REQ-SYS-182 hold as written; the 12 kHz injection of TC-SYS-101 trips with 2 482 Hz to spare; a fault during transmission is detected and RF ended in 46 ms (two 8 ms intervals, a 10 ms service period and the 20 ms RF-off of REQ-SYS-004) against 100 ms. The 482 Hz margin holds while FC0's accuracy at interval 12 is at most 560 Hz at the input (the dev-board known-clock acceptance limit, C-16a).
- **What each finalist needs.** R3 needs a reference the RP2350 can count independently of the synthesizer's programming. **A5** has it (the TG2520SMN on XA); it adds the GPIN1 route, which revision 5's block diagram lacked. **A4** has only the Adafruit module's own 25 MHz crystal, which no pin exposes; so **A4 needs the TCXO** (the former add-back AB2), fitted exactly as in A5 (module crystal removed, TCXO into XA through 10 nF). This is now a required A4 item (D-17), and its cost is in the roll-ups. The alternative, R1 with the CR deltas above, is not recommended: it would let a synthesizer error of up to about 28 kHz pass the check, against 9.5 kHz on R3; both routes catch the gross error HZ-008 names (5 W on 150 to 174 MHz).
- **The TCXO's second path (D-17).** The TG2520SMN is a clipped-sine TCXO (0.8 V peak to peak minimum into 10 kohm and 10 pF through a 10 nF DC cut: Epson TG2520SMN datasheet as summarized by a web search of 2026-09-29, not read directly; read at the gate with section 8.11 item 1), so GPIN1 takes it through a self-biased 74LVC1G17-class buffer with its own 10 nF coupling and a high-value bias divider (so that the TCXO's rated load is kept with the Si5351A's XA input in parallel; values in WP-PDR-20). The buffer is powered only while the ratio is counted in receive and is off in transmission, so the squared 25 MHz does not raise the 150.000 MHz line that is already one of the three spur lines over 25 uW (spur analysis). Open for WP-PDR-20 and WP-PDR-32: with the buffer off in transmission, the ratio used during an over is the last receive refresh, so the 1 ppm XOSC drift allocation must cover the longest over, or the ratio is refreshed in the gaps between elements at a level the spur plan admits.
- **Requests.** WP-PDR-20: the counter route R3 and the TCXO buffer in the clock plan for the chosen finalist (INSP-118 X-5). WP-PDR-35: the SW-SAFE threshold 5.0 kHz, the ratio refresh and its +/-67.5 ppm plausibility, DIED as disagreement, the lock-status read before PA_EN (the frequency-budget note's section 5 list). WP-PDR-36a: two GPIN pins (GPIO 20 and 22, or 12 and 14). WP-PDR-16b: the HZ-008 K7 wording for route R3 (the frequency-budget note's section 5 request, which this study now depends on).

**Revision 6: key-down sequence, frequency check and PA permit (INSP-118 findings 1 part 3 and 3; INSP-110 finding-23).** Two independent lines, both needed for RF, and a frequency check that finishes before the second is set:
- **TX_KEY** is the keyer's key line (SW-KEYER and SW-TXSEQ, GPIO, 4.7 kohm pull-down). **PA_EN** is a separate GPIO, also with a 4.7 kohm pull-down, written only by the safe-state manager (SW-SAFE) from its separately maintained PA-permit flag, which is set only when every prerequisite of 07 section 14.2 holds, including the counted frequency agreement, and cleared by `safe_state()`.
- **Hardware gate (D-18).** A three-input NAND (74LVC1G10 class) of TX_KEY, PA_EN and the monostable output Q (HZ-004 K5, whose own input is TX_KEY) drives, in hardware, (i) a clamp FET on the VGG node (A5) or the gate-bias node (A4), on whenever the NAND output is high, so the gate bias is held at 0 V at reset and whenever any of the three is low; and (ii) the gate of a P-FET that supplies the GVA-84+ alone, so the driver is unpowered on the same condition. The prescaler keeps its own transmit-only supply, switched by firmware at the changeover, because the frequency check must run before PA_EN is set. The existing wired-OR clamps of REQ-SYS-055, 180, 181 and 092 stay on the same node. A single stuck or corrupted line, TX_KEY or PA_EN, therefore leaves the driver unpowered and the gate bias clamped: about -67 dBm (A4) or -61 dBm (A5) at the antenna by the keying note's path estimate, not a transmission. With both lines up, the monostable still ends RF within 7.5 to 13 s and SW-SAFE can end it through PA_EN, so HZ-004 K5 and K8 stay two independent means.
- **D-11 restated.** The drive gating of D-11 (the GVA-84+ supply following TX_KEY) is this hardware gate, not a firmware write. CLK1 enable is a firmware I2C write to the Si5351A: a sequencing action, not a safety condition, and no longer called the second condition of REQ-SYS-120.
- **Sequence at the first element of an over** (t0 = the changeover command, after the 1 ms sampling and 2 ms make filter of REQ-SYS-160): at t0 the relay is commanded to TX, the prescaler is powered and PLL A is retuned to the carrier with CLK1 enabled (the I2C writes of the spur plan's C6); **1 ms is allocated** for the writes and the PLL to settle (the Si5351A relock time is not in the corpus; WP-PDR-32 confirms it); FC0 counts interval 12 from t0 + 1 ms to t0 + 5 ms; SW-SYNTH reads the lock status and SW-SAFE compares the count within the 2 ms software allocation, so the frequency prerequisite is known at **t0 + 7 ms**, and only then does SW-SAFE set PA_EN. TX_KEY rises at t0 + 8 ms (the GVA-84+ powers up and the bias clamp releases), and at **t0 + 10 ms** the reference clamp releases and the ramp starts, as before (relay operate 7 ms, bounce 0.5 ms, 2.5 ms margin). The check ends 3 ms before the ramp and 1 ms before the drive; the lead-in stays 10 ms (REQ-SYS-161, 12 ms) and 1 + 2 + 10 = 13 ms against REQ-SYS-160's 15 ms. If the dev-board check forces interval 13 at changeover, the check ends at t0 + 11 ms and the ramp moves to 11.5 ms, 0.5 ms inside the lead-in.
- **During the over.** CLK1 stays on from the changeover to the end of the hang time, so FC0 repeats interval 13 continuously (46 ms to RF off for a fault). For each element TX_KEY rises 2 ms before the ramp and falls 1 ms after it ends (the keying note's timing); between elements the driver is unpowered and the bias clamped by the gate, so the key-up level with CLK1 still running is about -67 dBm (A4) and -61 dBm (A5), under REQ-TX-014's -30 dBm. At the end of the over CLK1 is disabled and the relay returns to RX (REQ-SYS-183: about -117 and -111 dBm with CLK1 off; about -67 and -61 dBm even if a firmware fault left CLK1 on, under -57 dBm).
- **What changes for the keying analysis.** The keying note's REQ-TX-014 failure for A4 (-23 to -19 dBm) was the gate-off leakage with the driver powered; with the driver unpowered by TX_KEY in hardware that path loses the GVA-84+ gain and the unpowered part's isolation (20 dB, estimate). WP-PDR-22 reruns the key-up case with the gate (request with D-18).

**Revision 6: the REQ-SYS-181 cut-off for A4 and the AFT05 junction rating (INSP-118 finding-2).** HZ-003's thermistor-failure branch (the REQ-SYS-118 sensor reading cold, cause C4) is bounded only by the firmware-independent cut-off K9 (REQ-SYS-181, 95 C +/-3 C; 07 section 14.2 row i).
- **On the sink (revision 5) it does not bound A4:** the A4 sink settles at 84.6 C at the 45 C corner and the trip never acts (`trips.csv`, `hw_sink` nan for A4-DC), leaving the junction at 122.7 C nominal and up to about 141 to 143 C at the band top or with the adverse dissipation.
- **The AFT05MS004N rating, read for this revision** (NXP, then Freescale, datasheet AFT05MS004N Rev. 0, 7/2014, page 2): Table 1, operating junction temperature -40 to +150 C (notes: "Continuous use at maximum temperature will affect MTTF"), case operating temperature -40 to +150 C, total device dissipation 28 W at TC 25 C derated 0.23 W/C; Table 2, RthJC 4.4 C/W (case 79 C, 4.0 W CW, 7.5 V, 520 MHz); page 4, Figure 3, MTTF against junction temperature from 90 to 160 C. Read through the web-fetch tool, which cached the PDF (SHA-256 `84cd9fae...6dd036`); text extracted with pdftotext.
- **On the tab copper (TS-011 rule 14; D-6 revision 6)** NTC-2 reads the tab copper a little below the case: 4.5 K nominally at 5.55 W and 10.4 K with the sensor offset and lag adverse (`inhibit.csv`, A4-DC, `case_minus_reading_at_first_off`). The junction at the trip is then the trip reading plus that offset plus P x RthJC: **95 + 4.5 + 5.55 x 4.4 = 123.9 C nominally (126.9 C at the +3 C edge), and 98 + 10.4 + 6.86 x 4.84 = 141.6 C with every input adverse** (the all-adverse dissipation 6.86 W and a 10 % higher RthJC, as the thermal record's adverse case takes them). That is **8.4 K under the 150 C maximum** at the all-adverse corner and 23 to 26 K under it nominally. The bound holds whatever the case-to-ambient path does, because the sensor sits on the device side of it: a hotter box only makes the trip act sooner, and a lifted device or voided pad raises the reading with the junction (TS-011 rule 14). In the nominal steady state the tab reads about 93.8 C, just under the trip, so the cut-off may not act at all and the junction then stays at 122.7 C, under its own trip junction.
- **Result:** the tab cut-off bounds the adverse branch under the rating, so **no REQ-SYS-181 value delta is needed** for A4; the requirement's wording changes from "PA heat-sink temperature" to the PA sensing point of TS-011 rule 14 for A4 (section 8.10). The margin rests on the lumped model and on the NTC offset, which the bench NTC-offset measurement (thermal section 9.1 item 2) measures: if the measured tab-to-reading offset at 6.9 W exceeds about 18.8 K, the A4 trip junction would reach 150 C and REQ-SYS-181 would need a lower threshold for A4 by CR (a revisit condition, section 10). The junction would sit near 124 to 142 C only in the fault branch, not in normal use; the MTTF effect of that excursion is not claimed.
- **Common cause.** NTC-1 (REQ-SYS-118, E5 (e)) and NTC-2 then share the tab copper, as in TS-011 rule 14: routed apart, NTC-2 on its comparator (LM393 #1) and NTC-1 on the ADC; a detached device or a voided pad would mislead both, which the via-array inspection (RSK-006 step S5) and the TRR key-down test cover.
- **A5** keeps the cut-off on the sink: it acts at 9.6 min with the channel at 120.2 C (`trips.csv`), about 123 C at the 98 C edge, against the module's 175 C channel rating (INSP-112 finding-12 reading). Requests: the A4 values go to WP-PDR-16b (HZ-003 K9 sensing point) and to the REQ-SYS-181 writer with thermal request R-3.

**Through-hole at 144 MHz, honestly (transmitter and receiver research).**
- No through-hole VHF power device is in active production and stocked by a franchised distributor. RF Parts lists NOS parts with prices (RD06HVF1 USD 9.91, RD15HVF1 USD 14.91, 2N4427 USD 3.91), which meets the listed-price rule but is a finite supply; the RD06HVF1 gives 3.8 W at 7 V and 2.8 W at 6 V on its datasheet graph.
- The IRF510 has roughly 0 to 5 dB of power gain at 144 MHz; the BS170 and 2N7000 are DC switches here.
- The TO-92 J310 is listed (InterFET J310 at Mouser USD 5.83, 381 in stock; INSP-110 row 25); the SOT-23 MMBFJ310 (USD 0.23) is chosen on cost. The SA612 mixer is discontinued. A 146 MHz tuned circuit has no catalog through-hole coil.
- **Conclusion:** a 5 W 2 m radio from catalog stock needs a PA module or a handful of SOT-89 and SOT-23 parts, and 0805 or 1206 capacitors in the RF sections. Everything else is through-hole or a module.

**PA of A5: line-up and drive.**
- Si5351A CLK1 (3.3 V CMOS, drive 8 mA; into 50 ohm about +10.4 dBm at the fundamental, derived from a 1.65 Vpp square wave; up to about +12.9 dBm if the output resistance is 25 ohm) into a 3-pole drive LPF (fc about 170 MHz, one 1812SMS and two 1206 C0G), an input pad, the GVA-84+ (24.1 dB typical at 100 MHz, P1dB +20.4 dBm typical and +19.4 dBm minimum; datasheet Rev. F per the adversarial check), a 3 dB output pad, and the RA07M1317M module (Pin 20 mW nominal; stability specified for Pin 10 to 30 mW; 30 mW maximum rating).
- **Revision 5:** superseded by table R5-2 row 4: with the CLK1 coax interface the fixed pad gives 5.4 to 35.5 mW, so overdrive is not designed out; the select-on-test pad of section 8.14 replaces it. **Overdrive designed out:** the drive level is set by the Si5351 swing and two fixed pads, not by the GVA-84+ compressing. With an 18 dB input pad and GVA gain 22.5 to 25 dB, the module sees 10.5 to 14.4 dBm (11 to 28 mW) over the Si5351 range of +9 to +10.4 dBm; the GVA-84+ runs at about +16 dBm, 3.4 dB under its minimum P1dB. **LTspice check before the order (WP-PDR-21):** the drive chain with the Si5351 modelled as a 3.3 V square source of 25 to 50 ohm output resistance, the GVA-84+ S-parameters, the pads and the drive LPF; **pass: module input 10 to 30 mW at every corner (144 to 148 MHz, 25 and 50 ohm source, GVA gain 22.5 and 25 dB), 3f at the GVA input at least 25 dB below the fundamental.** If the 25 ohm corner exceeds 30 mW, the input pad grows by 1 dB and the low corner is re-checked.
- **Revision 5:** the keying analysis (table R5-2 row 9) shows this loop fails the keying requirements as written for both finalists, that the datasheet VGG transfer is steeper than stated here (1.0 W at 2.5 V, 7.1 W at 3.0 V, 8.4 W at 3.5 V: keying finding F1), and that the key-up statement "VGG under 1.2 V" does not hold (about 2.2 V, F2). The loop items of section 8.14 replace it. **Drive gating and key clicks.** Output power is set by VGG (datasheet graph at 7.2 V: about 0 W at 1.5 V, 2 W at 2.5 V, 4 W at 3.0 V, 7 W at 3.5 V; steep and nonlinear). A raised-cosine VGG would not give a raised-cosine envelope, so the ALC is a closed envelope loop: a 1N5711 detector on the LPF output side feeds an MCP6002 error amplifier whose reference is the firmware raised-cosine ramp (filtered PWM), and the loop drives VGG. **VGG clamp (revision 3, finding-14; resized in revision 4).** Datasheet maximum ratings (Jun. 2019, read 2026-09-27): VGG 4 V applies only at VDD 7.2 V or less with Pin 20 mW; Pout 10 W is rated at VGG 3.5 V or less; the stability guarantee covers Pout up to 8 W. Revision 2's 0.75 divider (3.83 V at VDD up to 8.4 V) was outside the first two conditions. Revision 3's 0.68 divider was sized on a 5.1 V LDO maximum that the datasheet does not give. The TI LM2940 datasheet (SNVS769J, section 6.5, 5 V option, read 2026-09-27) gives an output of 4.85 to 5.15 V at TJ 25 C and **4.75 to 5.25 V** over the recommended operating temperature range, and the MCP6002 output swings to its 5 V rail, so 0.68 reached 3.50 V at 25 C and 3.57 V over temperature, and about 3.68 V with 5 % resistors (INSP-110 iteration 3). VGG is now bounded by a **divider of ratio 0.654 from two 1 % resistors** of E24 values (for example 2.7 k over 5.1 k, or the same ratio at the impedance the WP-PDR-22 deck sets for the module's VGG input current): ratio 0.6493 to 0.6584 over both tolerances, so VGG is at most 5.25 x 0.6584 = **3.46 V** and at least 4.75 x 0.6493 = **3.08 V**, before the op-amp's output headroom, the module's gate current and any LM2940 dropout at the low pack end, all of which only lower it. The highest value is under the 3.5 V condition at every pack voltage and LDO temperature. The resistors must be 1 % (metal film): from the owner's assortment if it is 1 % (gate item 9), otherwise two 0805 1 % values inside the E2 row's USD 0 to 3.00 assortment-gap allowance, so no total changes. A reference independent of the 5 V bus was not taken: it adds a part, and the 1 % divider meets the condition. The datasheet gives nominal output at VGG 3 V typical and 3.5 V maximum, so a module at the high end of that spread may reach slightly less than saturation at the 6.4 V pack end with the clamp at its low end; that is carried in the REQ-SYS-012 risk (0.0 to 0.6 dB margin) and checked in WP-PDR-22 below. **Open-loop case.** If the loop opens (detector open, or the error amplifier at its rail), VGG sits at the clamp: with the clamp at 3.5 V or less the Pout 10 W rating condition holds, but at a full pack the module may exceed the 8 W stability range (revision 2's own figure is about 10.5 W at 8.4 V with VGG unbounded). Two bounds apply: the firmware plausibility check of REQ-SYS-156 (forward-power reading against the drive level) enters Fault-safe within 100 ms, and the 10 s hardware cutoff bounds the time. If the WP-PDR-22 run shows more than 8 W at the clamp at 8.4 V, the clamp is made pack-voltage dependent with the spare MCP6002 half and the spare 1N5711W already in the BOM (no cost), lowering it at high VDD while keeping the fixed-divider value at 6.4 V. **Revision 6:** the key-down sequence below is superseded by "Revision 6: key-down sequence, frequency check and PA permit" after table R5-3 in this section, which adds the pre-PA_EN frequency check (INSP-118 finding-1) and the PA_EN gating (finding-3); the relay timing and the parked integrator are unchanged. **Key-down sequence (revision 3, adversarial R-1).** The Omron G5V-2 datasheet (read 2026-09-27) gives an operate time of 7 ms maximum and a release time of 3 ms maximum; its bounce-time distribution graph shows about 0.3 to 0.5 ms of operate bounce (graph read). The ALC detector is on the LPF output, after relay pole A, so revision 2's 5 ms settle could start the ramp with the contacts still open: the loop would see no output, integrate VGG to the clamp, and step to full saturated power when the contacts closed (a key click, and at 8.4 V outside the stability conditions). Sequence now: relay to TX at t0; at t0 + 8 ms GVA-84+ bias on and CLK1 enabled with VGG still held at 0 V (module off); at **t0 + 10 ms** (7 ms operate plus 0.5 ms bounce plus 2.5 ms margin) the clamps on the envelope reference and the VGG node release and the ramp starts. Until then the error-amplifier integrator is parked at 0 V: the reference clamp holds the reference at 0 V and a bias resistor (owned) on the inverting input gives the error amplifier a small negative idle error, so its output rests at the 0 V rail, not wherever the offset drifts, and there is no wound-up integrator to release. As a check on a late or failed contact, firmware compares the detector reading with the reference at mid-ramp and enters Fault-safe if the reading lags (the REQ-SYS-156 range table, an L2 design item). The 10 ms lead-in is inside REQ-SYS-161's 12 ms and gives 1 ms sampling + 2 ms make filter + 10 ms = 13 ms against REQ-SYS-160's 15 ms. A4 uses the same relay and takes the same sequence. At key-up: the ramp takes VGG under 1.2 V (module output about 0 W), then after 1 ms CLK1 is disabled and the GVA-84+ bias switched off, then the relay returns to RX after the hang time. So the abrupt CLK1 edge happens only with the module off (backwave at most about -17 dBm estimated, under the 25 uW spur limit and never on the air in RX). The hardware clamps of REQ-SYS-055, 180, 181 and 092 are open-collector pulls on the envelope reference and VGG node (on at reset), and CLK1 enable is the second, independent condition of REQ-SYS-120 (revision 6: withdrawn; CLK1 enable is a firmware I2C action, and the second condition is the PA_EN line, section 7.3 revision 6 and D-18). **LTspice check before the order (WP-PDR-22):** the envelope loop with the VGG transfer as a table from the datasheet graphs (Pout versus VGG at 7.2 V; Pout versus VDD at VGG 3.5 V) at 6.4 and 8.4 V, the G5V-2 pole A modelled as a switch that closes at 7.5 ms (operate plus bounce, with 0.5 ms of bounce) and, as a fault case, at 12 ms, and the integrator's idle bias; **pass: 10 to 90 % rise and fall inside the set 3 to 8 ms (REQ-SYS-014), 26 dB bandwidth at most 350 Hz with continuous dits at 50 WPM (REQ-SYS-015, TC-SYS-013 method), overshoot at most 0.2 dB with the nominal contact timing, loop phase margin at least 45 degrees, VGG never above 3.5 V with the LM2940 output at 4.75 to 5.25 V (and in dropout at the 6.4 V end under key-down sag), the divider resistors at +/-1 %, the MCP6002 output headroom and the module's VGG input current modelled (revision 4, finding-14); the integrator output at 0 V at ramp start; with the contact closing at 12 ms (fault case), module output at most 8 W at every instant and the mid-ramp plausibility check tripping; open-loop case (detector open) at 8.4 V: module output at the clamp at most 8 W, or the pack-dependent clamp adopted; at 6.4 V the lowest clamp (3.08 V: LM2940 at 4.75 V and the lowest divider ratio) still gives at least 3.97 W at the SMA with the table's high-VGG module.**
- **RF off (REQ-SYS-183, -57 dBm).** In receive and in every ended or inhibited state, CLK1 is disabled, the GVA-84+ is unpowered and VGG is at 0 V, so nothing at the carrier frequency is generated; the relay's receive pole grounds the RX input. Designed out; **bench check before first on-air use:** tinySA at the monitor port with the radio in receive, inhibited and ended states: at most -97 dBm at the port (-57 dBm at the antenna).
- **Stability.** The module's stability is vendor-specified (spurious below -60 dBc) for VDD 4.0 to 9.2 V, Pin 10 to 30 mW, Pout up to 8 W by VGG control and load VSWR 4:1. The design keeps VDD at 5.5 to 8.4 V, Pin at 11 to 28 mW (LTspice check above), and VGG positive only while the drive is on (sequence above). The GVA-84+ is unconditionally stable (datasheet, per the adversarial check). The remaining exposure is board-level feedback around about 45 dB of gain; **bench check before first on-air use:** with the dummy load and at 6.0 and 8.4 V, (a) VGG at 0 V and CLK1 on: no output above -30 dBm at any frequency from 1 MHz to 1.5 GHz; (b) at each power step: no non-harmonic output within 20 dB of the 25 uW limit (the tinySA sweep of the harmonic check below).
- **Revision 5:** superseded by table R5-2 row 5: in steady key-down A5 does not reach 3.97 W at 6.4 V in any case (nominal 4.09 W, lowest corner 3.19 W at the LPF median), and the LPF loss is 0.74 dB median, 1.76 dB worst, not 0.4 dB. The "3.97 to 4.57 W" below is withdrawn. **Output power at the connector at the 6.4 V pack end (answers adversarial C2 for A5).** REQ-SYS-012 measures the pack in receive; at key-down the pack delivers about 2.0 A (module about 1.8 A plus the 5 V bus). Drain-feed resistance: two P28A cells 0.04 to 0.06 ohm (E, cell DCIR not read); two AO3400A at VGS about 6.4 V, 0.04 to 0.06 ohm (datasheet Rev 3.1: 19 typical, 32 mohm maximum at VGS 4.5 V, used as the bound); MF-R300 0.02 to 0.08 ohm (E); holder contacts 0.02 to 0.04 ohm (E); two DMP3099L 0.13 to 0.20 ohm (E, not read); chokes about 0.01 ohm. Total 0.26 to 0.45 ohm, a drop of 0.52 to 0.90 V: 5.5 to 5.9 V at the drain. From the datasheet graph (5.3 W at 6.0 V, Pin 20 mW, 155 MHz; graph read) scaled by V squared: 4.46 to 5.13 W at the module, minus 0.4 dB LPF and 0.1 dB relay loss: **3.97 to 4.57 W at the SMA, against the 3.97 W floor: met with 0.0 to 0.6 dB of margin (Low).** At 8.4 V the module could make about 10.5 W with VGG unbounded; the ALC holds 5 W, and the 3.46 V clamp and the REQ-SYS-156 check bound the open-loop case (drive gating above). REQ-SYS-012 is kept; the delta is conditional (section 8.10). Levers if the bench reads low: drive the module at 25 mW (still inside 10 to 30 mW), or replace the two DMP3099L by a lower-resistance P-FET pair. **Check before the order:** the datasheet maxima of DMP3099L RDS(on) at VGS -6 V and MF-R300 R1max read into the budget (owner price-check list item 8 or the author); **pass: total feed resistance at most 0.35 ohm, which gives at least 4.2 W at the SMA.**
- **Revision 5:** superseded by table R5-2 row 7 (LPF analysis): attenuation met with the BOM values; the 0.5 dB loss criterion fails in every build; the binding A5 case is 5 W at 8.4 V on the -17 dBc back-off estimate. **Harmonics.** Module maxima 2fo -25 dBc and 3fo -30 dBc at 6 W (datasheet limits; typical -37 and -63 dBc at 144 MHz, graph read). LPF: 7-pole Chebyshev, fc 165 MHz, ideal 47.9 dB at 288 MHz and 75.9 dB at 432 MHz (adversarial C8 recomputation). With 40 dB real at 2f and 35 dB at 3f (the REQ-SYS-018 allocations), the worst guaranteed case is -65 dBc at both, 12 dB inside the 53 dBc legal limit and 5 dB past the 60 dBc target. At the 0.5 W step, where VGG back-off can raise the harmonic ratio (discrete line-up data show -17 dBc at 0.3 W, pa-device-candidates F4), the legal limit is 43 dBc and -17 - 40 = -57 dBc. **LTspice check before the order (WP-PDR-21):** the LPF with Coilcraft 1812SMS SRF and Q models, 1206 pad and via parasitics; **pass: at least 40 dB at 288 to 296 MHz, 35 dB at 432 to 444 MHz, passband loss at most 0.5 dB.** **Bench check before first on-air use:** tinySA 9 kHz to 1.5 GHz through the monitor port at each step, 144.05 and 147.95 MHz, 6.4 and 8.4 V; **pass: every spur at most -56 dBm at the port (25 uW at the antenna) and at least 60 dB below the carrier at the 5 W step.**
- **Revision 5:** the clock table below is completed and replaced by `docs/design/analysis/spurs-ts012.md` section 4.1; its changes C1 to C8, L1 to L5, F1 and F3 are adopted in section 8.14 (clk_sys 96 MHz in every state in place of the 125 MHz below); the result is table R5-2 row 8. **Non-harmonic spurs in the LPF passband (adversarial C10; revision 4, adversarial R-3).** Designed out where possible: firmware powers down CLK0 and CLK2 in transmit, there is no I2C traffic in transmit, and the drive LPF sits between the Si5351 and the GVA-84+. Revision 3 also said that USB off in transmit (the VBUS inhibit, REQ-SYS-092) removes the 48 MHz x 3 product; that was wrong. The RP2350 ADC needs clk_adc at 48 MHz (RP2350 datasheet clock table, "Must be 48MHz", as cited in `docs/design/analysis/clock-plan.md` and ADR-031 item 5), and both A4 and A5 read the ADC in transmit (the ALC detector for the REQ-SYS-156 check and the mid-ramp plausibility check), so PLL_USB and clk_adc run while transmitting whether or not USB is on. Revision 3's "RP2350 at 125 MHz in transmit (no harmonic in 136 to 165 MHz)" holds for the harmonics but not the fundamental: 125 MHz is itself inside both low-pass passbands, as is ADR-031's 150 MHz. **Clocks running in transmit and their lines from 118 to 175 MHz** (both LPF passbands with margin; derived from the clock frequencies, Low):

  | Clock (source) | Lines from 118 to 175 MHz | Note |
  |---|---|---|
  | XOSC 12 MHz (Pico 2 crystal, clk_ref) | 120, 132, **144.000**, 156, 168 MHz | always on; 144.000 MHz is 50 kHz from a 144.050 MHz carrier |
  | clk_adc and PLL_USB, 48 MHz | **144.000** MHz (x 3) | on while the ADC samples; the same line as the 12 MHz x 12 |
  | clk_sys = clk_peri: 125 MHz in transmit (this study) or 150 MHz (ADR-031) | 125 or 150 MHz (fundamental) | at 125 MHz the QSPI clock (clk_sys / 4, 31.25 MHz) adds 125 and 156.25 MHz |
  | 25.000 MHz reference (A5 TCXO; A4 the Adafruit module's crystal) | 125, 150, 175 MHz | always on |
  | Prescaler /2 (72.0 to 74.0 MHz) and /4 (36.0 to 37.0 MHz), transmit only | the carrier itself only | 3/4 and 5/4 of the carrier fall at 108 to 111 and 180 to 185 MHz |
  | Prescaler /8 (18.0 to 18.5 MHz), transmit only | 126.0 to 129.5 MHz (7/8 of the carrier) and 162.0 to 166.5 MHz (9/8) | inside the drive-LPF passband; 9/8 at the output-LPF cutoff |
  | Dense-class PWM (audio and sidetone, envelope reference) and the Pico 2 switchers (RP2350 core regulator, RT6150) | a line at every multiple of each PWM frequency; supply-borne sidebands on the carrier | ADR-031 rules 3 and 8 |

  **Coupling budget.** 25 uW at the SMA is -16 dBm. The GVA-84+ and the module give about 45 dB from the GVA-84+ input to the SMA (taken at full in-band gain, which overstates the module's gain below 135 MHz), so a line may reach at most about **-61 dBm at the GVA-84+ input** for 97.307(e) and about **-68 dBm** for the REQ-SYS-018 60 dBc target at 5 W (-23 dBm at the SMA); ahead of the 18 dB input pad the limits are about 18 dB higher. **Pre-order check (WP-PDR-20 transmit clock-plan item, revision 4):** list every clock running in transmit with its lines from 118 to 175 MHz (the table above, completed for the chosen clk_sys and its QSPI and PWM dividers); reconcile the transmit clk_sys with ADR-031 (125 against 150 MHz; a clk_sys of 88 to 117 MHz puts neither its fundamental nor its harmonics in that range, though its dividers still need listing); choose how the ADC runs in transmit ((i) clk_adc continuous, accepting its share of the 144.000 MHz line, or (ii) clk_adc enabled only around conversion bursts at the rate the REQ-SYS-156 check needs, with whether PLL_USB can also stop between bursts read from the RP2350 datasheet); and give each line an estimated coupled level at the GVA-84+ input. **Pass: every line at most -68 dBm at the GVA-84+ input (estimate), or a layout, clock or firmware change named for it.** The 144.000 MHz line (12 MHz x 12 always, 48 MHz x 3 while the ADC runs) cannot be moved and is verified by the tinySA sweep above (same pass criterion). No cost; A4 has the same clock plan and takes the same item.
- **Revision 5:** superseded by table R5-2 rows 1 and 2 (WP-PDR-28): the in-situ sink resistance is 5.6 K/W (A5 with the design changes) to 7.2 K/W (as drawn), not 2.6 to 5.0; the flange contact face is 1.42 cm2, not 2.6 cm2 (interface about 0.5 K/W); the junction is 126.0 C with the design changes. The levers below (fin orientation, guard clearance) were run: neither closes REQ-SYS-112; the duty limit is the path, with a REQ-SYS-112 delta. **Junction temperature at the worst corner: an estimate, not a bound (revision 4, adversarial R-4; answers adversarial C19 for A5).** Corner: 45 C ambient, continuous key-down at 5 W at the SMA (5.6 W at the module after 0.5 dB of loss), pack at 8.4 V, ALC backing the module off. Efficiency: the datasheet minimum total efficiency is 45 % at 6 W and 7.2 V, so IDD is at most 1.85 A there; scaled class-B to 5.6 W (IDD proportional to output amplitude) that is 1.79 A, plus 10 % for the scaling error: **1.97 A** (derived). At full charge the drain sees 8.4 V minus the minimum feed drop (0.26 ohm x 1.97 A): 7.9 V. Dissipation 7.9 x 1.97 - 5.6 = **9.95 W** (8.54 W without the 10 %). Stage split: stage 1 1.5 W (the datasheet thermal-table condition), stage 2 the rest. Thermal path: stage-2 Rth(ch-case) 2.4 K/W, stage-1 4.5 K/W (datasheet); flange to sink through Wakefield 120 compound (4 g, in the BOM) on about 2.6 cm2, 0.2 to 0.4 K/W (derived for a 25 to 75 um bond line at 0.7 W/m K). The GVA-84+ (0.5 W) sits on the RF board in the vented bay (section 8.1), not on the sink. **Why revision 3's figure was not a bound.** The Boyd 530002B02500G's 2.6 K/W is a catalog free-air rating for the whole sink (distributors: 20 W at 60 C rise, radial fins). In this case part of the fin area and the inner face sit inside the vented bay, whose air revision 3 itself estimates near 60 C; the outside fins face a slotted guard 3 mm from their tips, which restricts natural convection; and the profile and flange face are unread (gate item 7). Revision 3 took 3.4 K/W (the rating plus a +30 % it called arbitrary), referred the whole sink to 45 C and called the result a bound. **Estimate (Low; revision 4):**

  | Case | Dissipation | Interface + sink | Case (flange) | Stage-2 channel | Stage-1 channel |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Nominal: catalog rating, whole sink in 45 C air | 8.54 W | 0.3 + 2.6 K/W | 69.8 C | 86.7 C | 76.5 C |
  | Revision 3 figure (+30 %) | 9.95 W | 0.4 + 3.4 K/W | 82.8 C | 103.1 C | 89.6 C |
  | About half the fin area effective (adversarial R-4) | 9.95 W | 0.4 + 5.0 K/W | 98.7 C | 119.0 C | 105.5 C |
  | Same, drain at a full 8.4 V with no feed drop (e.g. a bench supply) | 10.95 W | 0.4 + 5.0 K/W | 104.1 C | 126.8 C | 110.9 C |

  The stage-2 channel is therefore **about 87 to 119 C** at the corner against REQ-SYS-112's 110 C: not shown, so **REQ-SYS-112 is at risk for A5**, as it is for A4 (100 to 135 C). In the half-fin case the case exceeds the Mitsubishi 90 C guidance and the REQ-SYS-181 95 C sink trip would end the transmission (safe, but not the requirement). In service the 10 s cutoff (REQ-SYS-055) and CW duty make true continuous key-down unlikely, but REQ-SYS-112 takes the continuous corner as the steady-state bound of every duty cycle. **Check before the order (WP-PDR-28):** the thermal budget with the Boyd drawing (flat centre-channel face of at least 30 x 10 mm for the flange; mass; which fins face outside and which sit in the bay), the bay air temperature, and the guard clearance and slot area; **pass: stage-2 channel at most 110 C and case at most 90 C at the corner.** **No-cost levers first (WP-PDR-28 decides):** mount the sink so that the most fin area faces outside (profile-dependent), and widen the guard clearance from 3 mm to about 10 mm (+7 mm of case length, a C6 cost not scored here). **Fallback if it still fails (finding-11, revision 3): a transmit-duty limit at USD 0.** Firmware refuses a new key-down, with a Morse announcement, while the sink NTC reads above a set point fixed in WP-PDR-28 (below the 95 C REQ-SYS-181 hardware trip), so the time-average dissipation, not the key-down level, is limited. A power derate helps little: with VGG control the module's dissipation falls slowly with output (the class-B scaling of the estimate gives about 9.5 W at 4 W at the SMA, against 9.95 W at 5 W). The duty limit's cost is in requirements: REQ-SYS-112's continuous key-down corner would be replaced by a duty-limited corner (TBR), a KDR delta for the owner to accept by CR. A larger sink in the same envelope has no listed price: two more catalog reads today found only weaker TO-220 extrusions of the same size (Boyd 529802B02500G 3.7 K/W, Ohmite RA-T2X-64E 3.1 K/W; section 11), and a larger extrusion changes the case, so it is not carried as a priced line. The Boyd profile and flange face stay unread until gate item 7 (Mouser describes the part as 63.5 x 18.29 x 3.17 mm; Farnell and element14 give 41.91 x 63.5 x 25.4 mm).
- **Revision 5:** superseded by table R5-2 row 3: the guard of the end face only leaves the top and bottom fin tips reachable (64.2 C for A5, 49.7 C for A4, both FAIL); the wrap-around guard of section 8.14 passes for both. **Hand-hold surfaces (REQ-SYS-113) and PETG.** After 5 min at 25 C ambient (sink time constant about 105 to 155 s for 40 to 45 J/K), the sink rises 19 to 20 K nominally (44 to 45 C), up to 31 K at the revision-3 figure (56 C), and about 37 K (62 C) with half the fin area effective (revision 4, R-4: 9.95 W x 5.4 K/W x (1 - exp(-300 / 225 s))). **Designed out:** the fins sit behind a printed PETG finger guard (a slotted grille 3 mm clear of the fin tips, free filament) so that no fin is reachable, and no PETG part touches the sink: the sink is held by the RF board and metal standoffs with an air gap of at least 2 mm to the printed end frame, because the sink base reaches about 70 to 99 C at the 45 C continuous corner (revision 4 estimate), above PETG's 69 C heat-deflection temperature (TS-011 M8). **Check in WP-PDR-28 before the enclosure freeze:** pass: every PETG surface within 5 mm of the sink at most 60 C at the 45 C continuous corner, and the guard's outer surface at most 48 C at the 25 C, 5 min case; **bench check:** thermocouple on the guard and case after 5 min key-down at 25 C, at most 48 C.
- **Revision 5:** superseded by table R5-2 row 3: the main bay carries about 2.1 W (the feed parts add about 1.0 W; thermal finding T-2), and with the design changes A5's cells reach 50.7 C after 30 min at 50 % duty (PASS) and 58.9 C in a long session at the duty limit (FAIL). **Heat inside the case and the cells (revision 3; adversarial R-2 refuted revision 2's "heat outside the case").** Distributors describe the Boyd 530002B02500G as a double-centre-channel, radial-fin profile (finding-11). The module (flange about 70 to 99 C at the 45 C continuous corner, revision 4; plastic cap hotter), the sink's inner face (about 27 cm2), the RF board and part of the fin area are inside the PETG box. The adversarial lumped estimate: about 0.2 W/K from the 83 C inner face, 1.5 to 2 W of internal dissipation (LM2940 0.6 to 0.9 W, GVA-84+ 0.5 W, Pico) and about 0.13 W/K through the 2 mm PETG walls give **about 74 C interior air at 45 C ambient under continuous key-down and about 61 C at 50 % duty**. The 18650 cells, 13 mm from the sink zone on the same board, would pass their 60 C discharge limit, the LM393 cell 60 C trip would end transmissions in that corner, and the air would pass PETG's 69 C heat-deflection temperature. Revision 3 answers with layout, at no cost:
  1. **Vented PA bay.** A printed double-wall bulkhead (two 1.2 mm walls with a 3 mm air gap, at least 5 mm from the sink face) closes off the module and RF-board zone from the main bay. The RF board carries the GVA-84+ and the G5V-2 (its 5 V, 100 mA coil, 0.5 W, per the Omron datasheet), so both sit in the bay. The bay's top and bottom case walls are slotted as a chimney so its air exchanges with the outside; the slots on the fin side sit behind the finger guard. Only the DC, drive and coax leads pass the bulkhead.
  2. **Cells at the far end.** The two 1043P holders move to the antenna end of the main board's underside, about 40 mm from the sink face instead of 13 mm; the Pico 2 and the LM2940 take the sink end of the main bay.
  3. **Independent control kept.** The LM393 #2 cell 60 C trip (HZ-007) stays as the hardware backstop; at 45 C ambient under heavy sending it, not the PA, may be what ends a transmission, and the owner is told so (section 1, Q6).

  **Author's lumped estimate after the changes (Low):** main-bay dissipation 0.8 to 1.1 W (LM2940 0.6 to 0.9 W, Pico and op-amps about 0.2 W); main-bay walls about 0.11 W/K (85 % of the 0.13 W/K); the bulkhead about 0.035 W/K from a bay side taken at 60 C. Balance: (0.8 to 1.1) + 0.035 x (60 - T) = 0.11 x (T - 45), so **T = 54 to 56 C cell-bay air at the 45 C continuous key-down corner**, about 50 C at 50 % duty; cell self-heating adds about 1 to 2 K (2 A through an estimated 25 mohm per cell). The margin to 60 C is 2 to 5 K at a corner the 10 s cutoff makes unreachable in service, and about 8 to 9 K at 50 % duty: thin, and unanalyzed until WP-PDR-28. The bay air and bulkhead temperatures depend on the chimney flow, which is not estimated here. The bulkhead term takes the bay side at 60 C; with the sink face at up to 99 C (revision 4, R-4) the bay air may be hotter, and the result then rests on the bulkhead conductance (INSP-110 O-9: at 0.006 to 0.01 W/K it is much less sensitive to the bay temperature than at the 0.035 W/K used here); WP-PDR-28 reports the bay air temperature it computes. **Check before the order (WP-PDR-28, added to its criteria):** the thermal model of the two bays with the Boyd drawing (gate item 7); **pass, at 45 C ambient: (a) sending at 50 % key-down duty for 30 min: cell surface at most 55 C and main-bay air at most 55 C; (b) the REQ-SYS-112 continuous key-down corner: no cell above 60 C before the cell trip operates; (c) every PETG surface, bay walls and bulkhead included, at most 60 C.** If (a) fails: the transmit-duty limit above, then moving the LM2940 into the bay (its tab on the sink would add 0.6 to 0.9 W to the junction bound, so only after the junction check). **Bench check after assembly:** thermocouple on a cell after 30 min at 50 % duty into the dummy load at room temperature; the rise above ambient at most 10 K (the 55 C criterion at 45 C).
- **Load ruggedness (REQ-SYS-013).** The module is specified for load VSWR 20:1 at all phases at 9.2 V and 7 W without degradation, which covers 60 s at 80 % duty into 10:1.

**Revision 5:** A4's figures are in table R5-2 (junction 122.7 C; 3.06 W nominal at 6.4 V; surfaces fail as drawn, pass with the wrap guard, so "its hand-hold surfaces hold" below is not shown). **PA of A4 (revision 2; answers the adversarial refutations for the alternative).** Line-up as in revision 1 (Si5351, drive LPF, GVA-84+ at its P1dB limit, AFT05MS004NT1 with the NXP reference match). The match must be re-derived in LTspice from the NXP Zsource and Zload table (145 MHz: 9.05 + j12.43 and 2.56 - j0.54 ohm) for a 0.8 mm board, because the reference PCB is 0.51 mm FR4 with Coilcraft SQ inductors (adversarial C4); Coilcraft 0908SQ-25NJLC shows 0 stock at Mouser and USD 1.23 at Coilcraft direct today, so A4 keeps wound coils. Output at 6.4 V is about 3.2 W at the SMA (adversarial C2): REQ-SYS-012 needs a delta. The junction bound at 8.4 V with ALC back-off is 97 to 111 C on the revision-1 path values and about 100 to 135 C with realistic interface, spreading and sink derating (adversarial C19); the 0.8 mm board and the compound lower it but it is not shown under 110 C, so A4 also carries REQ-SYS-112 at risk. Its hand-hold surfaces hold (37 to 41 C, adversarial C20).

**Receiver (A5; A4 differs only in the mixer).**
- Chain: G5V-2 pole A, pole B grounding the RX input in transmit, 1N5711 clamp pair, BPF1 (2 wound air-coil resonators, owned 24 AWG), MMBFJ310 grounded gate, BPF2 (3 resonators), MMBFJ310 grounded gate, **diode-ring mixer** (four 1N5711W, two trifilar transformers on Fair-Rite 2843000202 BN-43-202 cores wound from the owned 24 AWG wire; LO 136 to 140 MHz low side on CLK0 at about +10 dBm), diplexer, 2N3904 post-mixer amplifier, 6-pole 500 Hz ladder of 8.000 MHz crystals matched on the NanoVNA (6 from 10), two 2N3904 IF stages with PWM-derived gain (AGC and volume), MMBFJ310 product detector (BFO on CLK2), NE5532 preamp and filter, summing with the PWM sidetone and menu tones, MCP6002 headphone buffers on 5 V, 220 uF coupling, attenuator, switched jack.
- Numbers: NF about 5.8 dB and MDS about -140 dBm (receiver research cascade with a diode ring; Low, +/-2 dB); -6 dB bandwidth about 470 to 525 Hz and -60 dB about 2.4 kHz (same-lot Monte Carlo); RMDR about 78 to 83 dB set by Si5351 phase noise (REQ-SYS-031 relaxation recorded); AGC after the crystal filter.
- **Revision 5:** superseded by table R5-2 row 10: "five resonators give well over 90 dB" was wrong (2 + 3 gives about 51 dB); a third section and five build conditions are needed for 70 dB; the 90 dB with at most 3 dB criterion is unattainable and is replaced (section 8.14). **Image (airband).** With an 8 MHz IF and low-side LO the image is 128 to 132 MHz, in the aeronautical AM band, 12 to 20 MHz from the passband. Five resonators give well over 90 dB ideal; board leakage typically caps image rejection near 60 to 70 dB (adversarial C14, estimate). **LTspice check before the order (WP-PDR-19):** BPF1 plus BPF2 with coil Q 100, 20 % coupling-capacitor tolerance and 5 nH ground-via inductance; **pass: at least 90 dB at 128 to 132 MHz and at most 3 dB passband loss** (the 20 dB above REQ-SYS-033's 70 dB is the leakage allowance). **Bench check after assembly:** NanoVNA S21 of the BPF chain at 130 MHz at the instrument floor (about 70 dB), and the tinySA generator at 130 MHz as a relative source (REQ-SYS-033 verification note).
- **Revision 5:** run: PASS for both (table R5-2 row 11). **Half-IF (RF - 4 MHz = 140 to 144 MHz).** A second-order product (2RF - 2LO = IF), so its level falls 2 dB per dB of input and depends on mixer balance. The balanced diode ring suppresses it far better than the single square-law JFET that revision 1 used (adversarial C14). **LTspice check before the order (WP-PDR-19):** front end and ring with a -70 dBm tone at RF - 4 MHz (70 dB above the -140 dBm MDS) against a -140 dBm tone at RF; **pass: the RF - 4 MHz tone gives an IF output no higher than the -140 dBm wanted tone (REQ-SYS-033, 70 dB), with the BPF attenuation at 140 MHz reported.**
- LO: the 18th BFO harmonic lands near 143.96 MHz, 36 kHz below the band (adversarial C16 holds). Frequency plan: integer divide-by-6 above 112.5 MHz, fractional PLL tuning (VCO 864 to 888 MHz and 816 to 840 MHz), step about 4 Hz; CLK0 and CLK1 share PLL A in time, BFO on PLL B.

## 8. Recommendation

- **Recommended alternative (06 section 14.5, revision 6): A4 with the required TCXO and A5's diode-ring mixer (U3)**, 300 of 500 (60 %), against A5 at 270 (54 %), **presented together with A2** (305, 61 %, not analysed). The 30-point lead over A5 is over the 25-point rule; A4 + U3 is first in all 16 weight runs between the two, and no single Low-cell move closes the gap (two can; section 6 revision 6). A2 is 5 points above it and the robustness verdict is Not robust, so 06 section 14.5 has the report say why the recommendation is not the highest total and put A2 to the owner with it (below and Q1). A4 scored with its JFET mixer is 285, only 15 over A5, which is why the ring is part of the recommendation, not an option.
- **How the ranking got here (net, INSP-110 finding-20).** Since revision 4 (A4 340, A5 335), net of the re-scoring A5 fell 65 points and the recommended A4 40. The discriminating analyses each moved against A5: heat in the case and the junction range (C8 -15), the drive window and the low-pack power (C5 -20), and the cost of the parts the analyses add (C1 -20). Two changes went the other way and narrowed A5's deficit: the wrap-around guard envelope that both need removed A4's C6 advantage (A4 -20, A5 -10), and C2 fell for A4 only (-5, and -5 more with the ring). Revision 6 then took 30 points from A4 on C3, because the TCXO it now needs is the flagged part A5 already carried, and gave back 20 on C5 with the ring and the TCXO together.
- **Why A4, on the evidence (section 7.3 table R5-2; plots named there):**
  - **Heat.** Neither finalist meets REQ-SYS-112 as written, but A5's junction range (113.6 to 145.9 C) lies wholly over 110 C and A4's (107.9 to 142.9 C) does not; A5 puts about twice the PA heat into the case and fails two box criteria outside their bands (continuous PETG, long-session cells), A4 none (`hardware/sim/thermal/results/2026-09-28-ts012-r2/margins_vs_band.png`, `tj_vs_duty.png`). A5's module case runs at 105.7 C against Mitsubishi's 90 C guidance.
  - **The module's advantages did not hold at the corners.** A5 does not reach 3.97 W at the 6.4 V end in any steady key-down case (`hardware/sim/tx-pa/results/2026-09-28-r2-s1-summary/a5_closure_margin.png`); its fixed drive pad leaves the module outside its stability conditions at 223 of 810 corners (`hardware/sim/tx-pa/results/2026-09-28-r1-d2-drive-a5/drive_a5_corners.png`); its harmonic guarantee does not cover the 5 W, 8.4 V state that binds its harmonic budget; and its envelope loop fails the first element at the worst-case sum where A4's passes (`hardware/sim/tx-keying/results/2026-09-28-r2-summary/windows.png`).
  - **Cost.** With the parts the analyses and the assurance conditions add (row E5, common), A5's worst case is USD 316.51, over the maximum before guards, and USD 299.83 after G1 and G2; the recommended A4's is 272.17, and its planning figure is USD 8.51 over the target.
  - **Safety conditions (INSP-118).** Both finalists now meet REQ-SYS-182 and REQ-SYS-154 as written on the same counter route, and both carry the same PA_EN gating. A5's REQ-SYS-181 cut-off keeps more margin (about 52 K on the sink against the module's 175 C) than A4's on the tab copper (about 8 K against 150 C at the all-adverse corner), the one assurance point in A5's favour; A4's bound still holds, and the bench NTC-offset measurement checks it before first on-air use.
- **What the recommended A4 costs the owner (stated plainly):** an End-of-Life PA with a spare in the order and a match still to be designed in LTspice (PA drive analysis C5); no vendor harmonic data, so its harmonic margin (5.5 to 7.5 dB on an assumed level) is confirmed only by the tinySA sweep after the owner buys the tinySA; low-pack power well short of REQ-SYS-012 (3.06 W nominal, 1.69 W at the lowest corner at 6.4 V; `hardware/sim/tx-pa/results/2026-09-28-r2-p2-power-a4/power_a4_temperature.png`), so a larger REQ-SYS-012 delta than revision 4 stated; the leadless TCXO (heat gun and flux, and removing the Adafruit module's crystal), now required; a REQ-TX-014 restatement (the drive gating in the key path, no cost, with an HZ-004 note); the REQ-SYS-181 sensor on the tab copper with an 8 K margin at the adverse corner. Six Red risks, against A5's eight (section 7.1).
- **Why not A5:** it would buy about 1.3 dB more low-pack output (still short of REQ-SYS-012), the vendor-guaranteed stability and harmonic limits at their stated conditions, a module in current production and a wider REQ-SYS-181 margin, for USD 40.32 more at planning, twice the heat in the box, a worst case over USD 300 before guards (USD 0.17 inside after them) and a harder first-element envelope. If the owner values the production-status PA over these, A5 remains buildable with the same REQ-SYS-012 and REQ-SYS-112 deltas and the guard ladder at the gate.
- **Why not A2, although it scores 305 (06 section 14.5; INSP-110 finding-19):** it was never analysed, and half its cells are Low-confidence architect estimates of the kind the analyses moved down for both analysed finalists (with their guard envelope it would total 275 to 295, below 300); it has three flagged parts, a new-old-stock PA pair whose RD07MUS2B is "no longer available for export", an LCSC-only charger chain whose merge is unverified, and the Catastrophic HZ-002 charger chain back in the box. What it offers that the finalists do not is in-radio USB charging. The analysis that would settle its cells is named in section 6 revision 6 item 4 (about two days, no money). The study recommends against running it and asks the owner to confirm the choice set as A4 and A5, or to order that analysis first (Q1).
- **Conditions (either choice):** the ordering gate of section 8.4; the design items of section 8.14, including D-6, D-17 and D-18 of revision 6; the owner decisions listed after Q1 in section 8.13 (REQ-SYS-112 and REQ-SYS-012 deltas, LPF values and loss criterion, REQ-TX-009 to 011 values, REQ-TX-014 condition with its HZ-004 note, REQ-SYS-181 sensing point, receiver front-end redesign with 2 + 3 + 4); the open items of table R5-3 (feed-resistance reads, the in-situ sink and NTC-offset measurement, the Si5351A relock time, the receiver re-review, INSP-110 iteration 3 re-issue 2 and INSP-118 iteration 2); no transmission on the air before the tinySA sweep.
- **Scope of sections 8.1 to 8.12 (revision 8, after the decision):** the bullets above are the recommendation as it went to the owner (revision 6) and stay as presented. The owner chose A5 (section 10), so revision 8 re-issues sections 8.1 to 8.12 for **A5, the chosen design**, as section 10 "Records produced" and WP-PDR-54 require: they now carry the design items of section 8.14 in their A5 form, the receiver figures of `docs/design/analysis/rx-bpf-ts012.md` revision 4 (INSP-110 finding-26: 75.37 dB over REQ-SYS-114, alignment acceptance +/-0.62 %, limit +/-0.64 %), and the envelope, mass and requirement values of the analyses. Readings that applied only to A4 (the AFT05 match, the 30 ppm frequency rows, the tab-copper REQ-SYS-181 sensing point, the A4 roll-ups and the A4 values in the delta rows) are removed where they would be read as part of the A5 design; A4 is named only where a comparison is the point (section 8.2) or where the A4 part is excluded (guard G6). No criterion, weight, score or roll-up total changes. Revision 6 had these sections detail A5 with A4's differences in sections 3.2, 7.1, 7.3 and 8.4, and planned a re-issue for A4 had the owner chosen it.

### 8.1 A5 architecture (block diagram)

Re-issued in revision 8 with the section 8.14 items (D-numbers in brackets). Each item's values stay in the record named in section 8.14 until the schematic (WP-PDR-37) carries them.

```
                     +----------------------------- Pico 2 (RP2350, Rust) ------------------------------+
                     | I2C | PWM: envelope ref, feedforward VGG 122 kHz (D-10), IF gain, relay hold     |
                     | ADC: ALC det, pot x2, AGC env, cell voltage, cell NTC, flange NTC (D-6) [note 4] |
                     | PWM audio (sidetone + Morse menu) | key/paddle | 2 buttons | LED                 |
                     | FC0: GPIN0 <- /8 prescaler (TX), GPIN1 <- TCXO squaring stage (RX) (D-17)        |
                     | TX_KEY, PA_EN (both 4.7 k pull-down) -> 3-input NAND with cutoff Q (D-18)        |
                     +---+----------+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                         |          |
 Adafruit 2045 Si5351A, 25 MHz crystal removed, Epson TG2520SMN TCXO into XA (10 nF); TCXO also -> 10 nF ->
   squaring stage powered in RX only (74LVC1G17 class named; see note 3) -> GPIN1 (route R3)
  CLK1 TX 144-148 -> 5-15 cm 50 ohm coax (D-8) -+-> 3-pole drive LPF, or drive bandpass (D-13)
                   |                             -> select-on-test pi pad 13.5-22.0 dB (D-7)
                   |                             -> GVA-84+ (its own supply P-FET, gated by the NAND; D-18, note 2)
                   |                             -> 3 dB pad -> RA07M1317M (flange on the sink, compound)
                   |   VGG <- pack-dependent clamp 3.30-3.50 V at 6.4 V (D-9) <- MCP6002 loop: trim integrator,
                   |          feedforward VGG, raised-cosine ref predistorted by the detector law (D-10)
                   |                              ^ biased 1N5711 detector with reference diode on the LPF output
                   |   clamps on the ref/VGG node (wired-OR, on at reset): NAND(TX_KEY, PA_EN, Q) clamp FET,
                   |          LM393 #2 10 s cutoff, LM393 #1 150-180 s backstop,
                   |          LM393 #1 95 C trip on the sink NTC (REQ-SYS-181), LM393 #2 cell 60 C, VBUS inhibit
                   +-> 100 pF, 47 ohm, 12 k / 9.1 k bias -> 74LVC1G80GV x3 (/8, 18.0-18.5 MHz; supply switched by
                       firmware at the changeover, so the frequency check runs before PA_EN) -> 470 ohm -> GPIN0
 ANT SMA edge (far end face) <-- 7-pole LPF (Coilcraft 1812SMS 2 %, 1206 C0G 2 %, trap footprint; D-14)
                             <-- + 40 dB tap -> monitor SMA
                             <-- G5V-2 pole A (TX / RX); pole B grounds the RX input in TX; coil held by PWM (D-5)
 RX: 1N5711 clamps -> BPF1 (2) -> J310 GG -> BPF2 (3) -> J310 GG -> BPF3 (4) -> diode ring (4 x 1N5711,
     2 x BN-43-202; CLK0 LO)   [2 + 3 + 4 at IF 8 MHz, internal ports matched to VSWR 1.2, fenced cells; D-15]
     -> diplexer -> 2N3904 -> 6-pole 500 Hz ladder (8.000 MHz, 6 of 10) -> 2N3904 IF x2 (PWM gain)
     -> J310 product det. (CLK2 BFO) -> NE5532 -> sum with PWM tones -> MCP6002 L/R -> 220 uF -> atten -> jack
 Power: 2 x P28A in 1043P holders -> S-8252AAO + 2 x AO3400A (pack negative) -> MF-R300 -> 2 x DMP3099L
        reverse/rail switch (EG1218 drives the gates) -> module drain; LM2940-5 -> 5 V bus; 3rd DMP3099L -> TX 5 V;
        supply filters F1 and F3 (D-12)
 Charging: cells out of the radio, XTAR MC1 (1 bay) from the owner's USB adapter. USB on the radio is for firmware only.
 Clocks in TX (D-12): clk_sys = clk_peri = 96 MHz, clk_adc in bursts, PLL_USB off unless enumerated,
        PLL B parked, CLK0 and CLK2 off, I2C only in the lead-in.
```

Notes to the diagram (revision 8):
1. **Thermal protection (D-6).** Three NTCs: the cell NTC (LM393 #2, 60 C trip; ADC), the REQ-SYS-118 inhibit NTC on the module flange contact face (about 81 C, pending the bench offset measurement; ADC; row E5 (e)), and the REQ-SYS-181 backstop NTC on the sink (LM393 #1, 95 C +/-3 C, routed apart from the ADC path), which acts with the module channel at about 120 to 123 C against its 175 C rating. The alternative to the flange NTC is a firmware duty limit that refuses a new key-down above 79.4 C on a sink NTC.
2. **Open lien on the PA permit gate (INSP-110 finding-24, INSP-118 finding-9).** The gate's supply rail, its level interface to the 5 V P-FET that feeds the GVA-84+, and its unpowered state are not stated. As drawn, a 3.3 V NAND output may not turn off the DMP3099L (VGS about -1.7 V against a threshold of -1.0 to -2.1 V), and the key-up level of about -61 dBm rests on the driver being unpowered. REQ-SYS-120 is not affected: the clamp FET holds the VGG at 0 V on its own. The fix (for example an N-FET level shifter with a pull-up to 5 V, cents inside row E5 (g)) is due with the WP-PDR-22 key-up rerun.
3. **Open lien on the TCXO squaring stage (INSP-110 finding-25).** A 74LVC1G17 is a Schmitt buffer and cannot be self-biased; with a fixed bias it is not shown to toggle at the TCXO's 0.8 V peak-to-peak minimum output (itself from a search summary, not read). A failure to count reads as DIED (no RF), so the fault is safe but costs availability. WP-PDR-20 names a stage that works at that level (for example a self-biased unbuffered inverter or a comparator), still powered only in receive; cents inside row E5 (g).
4. **Analog inputs (pin map, WP-PDR-36a).** The diagram lists seven analog signals, and D-10 asks for one more ADC channel. The RP2350 on the Pico 2 brings out fewer ADC inputs than that (four on the RP2350A package, one of them used by the Pico 2 for its supply sense, as the author recalls; not re-read for this revision), so the pin map needs a multiplexer, a shared channel or a changed allocation. Not a cost change at this stage; the author flags it for WP-PDR-36a and WP-PDR-37.

Boards: two JLCPCB 2-layer HASL designs, 5 of each, each within 100 x 100 mm: the main board 64 x 100 mm, 1.6 mm (cells in holders on the bottom at the antenna end, away from the sink; Pico 2 soldered flat by its castellations and the LM2940 at the sink end of the main bay), and the RF board about 40 x 35 mm (drive filter, pads, GVA-84+ and its gate, relay, output LPF, detector, 40 dB tap). **Heat sink and PA bay (D-1 to D-4):** the Boyd 530002B02500G sits wholly outside the case end wall, with the module in its inner channel facing the case; the RF board enters the 17.02 mm channel as a tongue at most 6.5 mm wide, and the module's leads are soldered to it after the module is screwed to the sink (datasheet mounting order; M2.6 screws with nuts behind the 1.57 mm web). The end wall facing the sink is a spare JLCPCB board (FR4, copper toward the sink) in a PETG frame, 5 mm from the inner fin tips; no PETG part touches the sink. A printed wrap-around finger guard covers the fins (end face 10 mm from the fin tips; top, bottom and profile ends 3 mm; 2 mm slotted grille checked with the test finger). Inside the case the RF board and the module leads sit in a vented PA bay with 5 cm2 slots top and bottom, behind a printed double-wall bulkhead; the main bay has 2 cm2 slots in the bottom and sides and none on top (IPX2). Receiver and synthesizer fences are cut from spare boards; the BPF sections are fenced cells. The owner's perfboard is for bench prototypes of the audio chain, the Morse UI and the safety timers.

| Upgrade | Capped cost | Retired or changed (A4 -> A5) | Added or raised | Net points | USD per point |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| U1 module PA | 36.88 | harmonic data and match 16 (Red) -> residual harmonic 8 (+8); REQ-TX-014 6 -> 6 (0); junction 20 -> 20 (0); REQ-SYS-012 15 -> 15 (0); end of life 8 -> single seller 8 (0) | envelope loop 6 -> 9 (-3); cell heating 10 -> 15 (-5); PETG, bay and surfaces 9 -> 12 (-3); drive window 12, new (-12) | 8 - 3 - 5 - 3 - 12 = **-15** | none (net rise) |

On the section 7.1 scores A5's module trades A4's harmonic-data Red for thermal, drive-window and cost risk; its case rests on the vendor-guaranteed harmonic and stability limits (scored in C5, not in the risk count) and, for the owner, on the parts being in production (section 10). The risks A5 carries into the PDR design work are the section 7.1 A5 rows; WP-PDR-18 enters them in the register. The revision 4 and revision 5 versions of this table (+6 points at USD 7.85; -1 point for USD 47.07) are superseded and kept in the revision 7 text (`6497900`). The matrix does not use these points.

Not bought, with reason: a spare RA07M1317M (USD 33.25; breaks the worst case; gate add-back AB-A); in-radio charging (section 3.2); a larger sink (no listed part found in the envelope; WP-PDR-28 fallback only).

### 8.4 Cost roll-up, ordering gate, guards and add-backs

**A5** (revision 6 figures; unchanged by revisions 7 and 8).

| Line | Low | Planning (midpoints) | Worst |
|---|---|---|---|
| Listed parts (Mouser 84.31 + RA07M1317M 28.91 + cells 11.98 + charger 4.99 + boards 4.00) | 134.19 | 134.19 | 134.19 |
| Estimated parts (rows E1 to E4) | 6.58 | 13.83 | 21.08 |
| Analysis-driven parts (row E5 with item (g)) | 5.49 | 12.31 | 19.13 |
| Shipping (Mouser, RF Parts, 18650BatteryStore, JLCPCB) | 32.00 | 45.50 | 59.00 |
| Board duty | 1.40 | 2.55 | 26.83 |
| Mouser tariff pass-through | 4.00 | 8.00 | 15.00 |
| **Subtotal** | **183.66** | **216.38** | **275.23** |
| Contingency 15 % | 27.54 | 32.45 | 41.28 |
| **Total, sales tax excluded, capped** | **211.20** | **248.83** | **316.51** |
| Against the USD 200 target | +11.20 | +48.83 | +116.51 |
| Margin to the USD 300 maximum | 88.80 | 51.17 | **-16.51** |

**A5's stacked worst case is over the USD 300 maximum by USD 16.51.** It stacks the high Mouser shipping (USD 8.00) with a merchandise total above the USD 100 free-shipping threshold, which guard G1 removes (-9.20, 307.31); G2 (the owner's knobs and hardware, -7.48) then gives **299.83**, USD 0.17 inside the maximum. Without the optional items of E5 (f) the worst case is 309.38, still over before the guards. The J310 port matching parts (row E5 (a) note) are not in these figures; one such value alone (USD 0.26 to 0.58 capped) exceeds the USD 0.17 left after G1 and G2, so G3 and G4 are the likely next steps if the gate reads come in at the worst case. The order proceeds only on the recomputed gate figure. Earlier totals: revision 4 (without E5) 204.89 / 234.68 / 294.51; revision 5 (E5 without (g)) 210.86 / 247.80 / 314.78. The A4 roll-ups of revisions 2 to 6 are in section 1 and the revision 7 text (`6497900`); they no longer bear on the order.

**Ordering gate (before anything is ordered; OD-42).** The owner reads in a browser, without checking out: (1) the Mouser cart with every row of section 8.3: merchandise total, the shipping charge and any tariff line; (2) the RF Parts cart for one RA07M1317M-501: stock and shipping to the owner's address; (3) the JLCPCB quote for the two designs with the cheapest US shipping that prepays duty, the duty shown, and whether the duty is charged on the boards or on the whole order; (4) the 18650BatteryStore cart (cells and MC1) shipping. Claude then recomputes the planning and worst totals with the verified numbers. **Contingency basis (revision 3, finding-16):** the gate applies the M1 basis unchanged, 15 % contingency on **every** line, verified or estimated, so the USD 300 test at the gate is the same test the owner was shown in section 4.1. The reason: once prices are verified the contingency no longer covers price error, but it still covers build-time growth that no gate read removes: a module or driver blown at first power-on (D12; a replacement module costs about USD 33 to 47 with shipping), re-ordered passives, a second board order. Dropping it on verified lines would loosen the test by up to about USD 20 (15 % of the USD 134.19 listed parts) against a stacked worst case already USD 16.51 over. The order proceeds only if the recomputed worst case, with contingency on every line, is at or below USD 300; the recomputed planning figure is reported against the USD 200 target. The gate also reads the lifecycle flag of every row (section 8.11 item 1): a part not Active triggers the section 10 revisit condition (b). Rounding (observation O-1): capped totals are shown truncated to the cent (the worst case is 316.5145, shown as 316.51).

**Guards, applied in order if the recomputed worst case exceeds USD 300** (capped values):
- **G1** free Mouser shipping: if the verified merchandise is within the shipping charge of the threshold, add gate add-back AB-B or spares until it crosses: up to -9.20.
- **G2** owner's knobs and hardware (rows E3, E4): up to -7.48.
- **G3** fold the RF board into a 70 x 100 mm main board (one JLCPCB design, case 6 mm wider; the tongue into the sink channel of D-1 is then cut from the main board): -2.30 plus the duty on one board.
- **G4** drop the spare GVA-84+: -3.44.
- **G5** (withdrawn in revision 6) drop the TCXO: not available without REQ-SYS-182 and REQ-SYS-154 deltas by CR (at least 16.1 and 27.7 kHz; section 7.3, revision 6), which are not proposed.
- **G6** (excluded since the decision) revert the PA to A4's AFT05 (-36.88): it brings back the End-of-Life part that the owner's condition excludes (section 10 condition 1).
- If the worst case is still over USD 300 after G1 to G4, the owner decides between not ordering and a change that reopens this study; nothing is ordered until then.

**Add-backs, in order, if the verified worst case leaves room** (capped values):
- **AB-A** spare RA07M1317M-501: +33.25. Only if the recomputed worst case with it stays at or below USD 300 (Q7).
- **AB-B** two-bay XTAR MC2 in place of the MC1 when back in stock: +2.30 net (sold out on 2026-09-27).

### 8.5 Envelope and mass (estimates, A5)

Re-issued in revision 8 on the thermal record's design changes D-1 to D-4 (`docs/design/analysis/thermal-ts012.md` section 8). No case CAD exists yet; WP-PDR-39 draws it and WP-PDR-29 budgets it.

- **Plan.** Body width: 64 mm board + 2 mm clearance + two 2 mm walls = 70 mm. Length: wrap-around guard end face 10 mm clear of the fin tips plus its 2 mm slotted grille, 25.4 mm sink wholly outside the case, 5 mm gap and the 1.6 mm FR4 end wall (D-1, D-2), 13 mm module and RF-board zone (the vented PA bay), about 5 mm of double-wall bulkhead (two 1.2 mm walls, 3 mm gap), 100 mm main board, 2 mm clearance, 2 mm wall: **about 167 mm**. The edge SMA adds about 7 mm at the far end face (174 mm), where the key and phones jacks, the monitor SMA and the Pico 2 micro-USB also sit. Moving the Pico 2 to the sink end puts its micro-USB on a side wall instead (layout item for WP-PDR-37 and 39).
- **Height.** Body: 2 mm wall, about 21 mm of cells in holders, 1 mm gap, 1.6 mm board, at most 12 mm of parts (G5V-2 about 11.5 mm; Si5351 module on its header about 9 mm), 1 mm gap, 2 mm wall: about 40.6 mm, so the body is **about 42 mm** to match the 41.9 mm sink. At the sink end the guard adds 3 mm of clearance and a 2 mm grille top and bottom: **about 52 mm**. Width at the sink end: 63.5 mm sink plus 5 mm each side: **about 74 mm**.
- **Envelope: about 167 x 74 x 52 mm at the sink end, body 70 x 42 mm (174 mm long with the SMA)** against REQ-SYS-103 (140 x 70 x 40 mm, TBR): +27 mm length (+34 mm with the SMA), and at the sink end +4 mm width and +12 mm height. Revision 4 had 153 x 70 x 42 mm, with the guard on the end face only; that guard fails REQ-SYS-113 (64.2 C at 5 min for A5), so the wrap-around guard is needed.
- **Board area.** Main board: about 58 cm2 of receiver, audio, control and power blocks plus about 3 cm2 of grafts (second LM393, dividers, second button) on 64 cm2: tight, and the third BPF section (four resonators, fenced) adds to it. The RF board carries the transmit chain and enters the sink channel as a tongue at most 6.5 mm wide (D-1). Fallback: a 70 x 100 mm main board (still within the JLCPCB 100 x 100 mm price), body 76 mm wide.
- **Mass: about 311 to 371 g** against REQ-SYS-102 (350 g, TBR): cells 92 to 96 g; holders 12 to 16 g; boards 23 g; components and wiring 62 to 77 g; Boyd sink 52 to 62 g (read at 57 g for revision 5); PETG shell with 2 mm walls 55 to 70 g; bulkhead and longer shell 3 to 5 g; wrap-around guard 8 to 16 g (the end-face guard's 5 to 8 g plus 3 to 8 g); screws and standoffs 4 to 6 g. Up to 21 g over at the high end.

### 8.7 Morse-code audio user interface (owner direction 8)

**Controls.** Two push buttons (B3F-1052: MENU and ALT), two owner potentiometers on ADC inputs (pot 1: fine tuning window of +/-5 kHz around the entered centre, about 10 to 20 Hz resolution with hysteresis; pot 2: volume), the key or paddle on the key jack, and the power switch. The Pico 2 on-board LED, behind a light pipe, shows transmit and blinks fault codes.

**Sequence (the owner's words, implemented).**
1. A short press of MENU enters the menu. Transmit is disarmed: the key line goes to the decoder, and only the sidetone path sounds.
2. The radio sends the menu letters in Morse through the sidetone path, at the keyer speed, with the tone level held under the headphone ceiling (REQ-SYS-071).
3. The operator sends one letter on the key or paddle to pick an item, for example: F frequency, P power step, S keyer speed, K key mode (straight, iambic A, iambic B), T tone pitch, C call sign, B battery and status read-out, X exit.
4. For a number, the operator sends digits (for F: kHz within the band, for example 144050); the paddle can also step a value (dit down, dah up) with a step size set in the menu.
5. The radio reads the value back in Morse.
6. The operator sends R to confirm or N to reject (the value is not applied on N). No entry for 20 s (TBR) exits without change.

**Rules.**
- Two menu levels at most from menu entry (REQ-SYS-062).
- The 5 W step needs the read-back and an R (REQ-SYS-063); a first entry after power-on starts at a lower step.
- A long press of MENU outside the menu sends the status in Morse (frequency, power step, key mode, speed, battery), replacing the status display (REQ-SYS-060).
- **Key-mode selection without the key (REQ-SYS-163, finding-7):** holding ALT for 2 s steps the key mode (straight, iambic A, iambic B) and the radio announces the new mode in Morse; this path uses the buttons only, so it works while any key input reads closed.
- **Guest lock (REQ-SYS-066, finding-7):** MENU and ALT held together for 3 s, then an R on a third press of MENU, sets or releases the guest lock.
- The call sign is sent at power-on and when the headphones are plugged in (REQ-SYS-006).
- Faults are announced in Morse within 1 s through the headphones and shown by an LED blink code without them (REQ-SYS-067, 077).
- Low battery, the identification reminder and the separation reminder are announced (REQ-SYS-068, 069, 096).
- The menu override command path stays safety-critical (SRR decision 9); the menu never clears a hardware clamp, and it never writes PA_EN (only the safe-state manager does, D-18).
- Straight-key decoding is adaptive (the highest firmware risk); paddle decoding is exact; if the straight-key decoder proves unreliable, menu entry is paddle-only.

**Firmware scope added (WP-PDR-35, 41):** Morse sender on the PWM tone path, adaptive decoder, menu state machine with R/N confirmation and time-out, number entry and read-back, pot reading with hysteresis, status and fault announcements, LED codes; frequency verification on the RP2350 FC0 counter with two GPIN inputs (route R3, D-17: the carrier /8 before PA_EN and during transmission, the TCXO ratio in receive with its +/-67.5 ppm plausibility, threshold 5.0 kHz, the prescaler supply switched at the changeover and the squaring stage powered in receive only); the PA_EN permit output of the safe-state manager (D-18); the envelope reference ramp predistorted by the detector law, the feedforward VGG table on a 122 kHz PWM channel, the trim integrator with per-element capture and stored trim, the low-pack setpoint limit of 4.0 W at 6.4 V, per-over zero calibration, per-unit feedforward calibration, NTC compensation and the detector tap switching at the 0.5 and 1 W steps (D-10); transmit sequencing (relay, PLL A retune and CLK1, the interval 12 frequency check, PA_EN, TX_KEY, ramp; the relay coil held by PWM after pull-in, D-5); the mid-ramp detector plausibility check (REQ-SYS-156); the transmit clock plan (D-12); the REQ-SYS-118 inhibit on the flange NTC or the firmware duty limit (D-6); AGC control voltage; Si5351 I2C driver with the TCXO reference. Removed: display driver and encoder drivers. The audio stays analog, so no ADC audio path or DSP is added.

**Hazard notes.** HZ-004 (stuck transmission): transmit is disarmed in the menu and the hardware cutoffs stay independent; RF needs both TX_KEY and PA_EN through the hardware gate (D-18). HZ-005 (hearing): every menu tone passes through the same capped path as the sidetone.

Also given up, by requirement delta rather than by descope: continuous key-down at 45 C ambient (REQ-SYS-112 moves to a duty-limited corner enforced by the REQ-SYS-118 inhibit; section 8.10, follow-on decision 1), and 5 W +/-1 dB at the 6.4 V pack end (REQ-SYS-012 to +1/-1.5 dB there; section 8.10, follow-on decision 2). No new descope number is taken, so the D-numbers of section 8.14 stay distinct.
