### 8.10 Requirement deltas (for the re-baseline CR; A5)

Current values from `docs/requirements/sys/requirements.json`, `docs/requirements/tx/requirements.json` and `docs/requirements/sw/sw-keyer/requirements.json` at HEAD. "Keep" means the value is unchanged; "at risk" names what the PDR analysis must show. Revision 8 states every row for A5 only; the A4 values of revisions 2 to 6 are in the revision 7 text (`6497900`) and do not go to the re-baseline CR (section 10 condition 2).

**User interface (display to Morse, owner direction 8).**

| REQ | Current | Proposed |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-SYS-006 | Display the call sign after every power-on | Announce the call sign in Morse at power-on and at headphone insertion |
| REQ-SYS-044 | Hang time in dits at the displayed speed | At the set speed |
| REQ-SYS-057 | Exactly two rotary knobs with push and two push buttons | Two push buttons, two potentiometer controls (tuning fine window, volume) and the key or paddle for menu entry, besides the power switch |
| REQ-SYS-058 | One step per tuning-knob detent, 10 Hz to 10 kHz with rotation rate | Morse direct frequency entry, a pot fine window of +/-5 kHz at 20 Hz resolution or better, and a paddle step mode of 10 Hz to 10 kHz |
| REQ-SYS-059 | Volume knob, at least 32 steps | Potentiometer read by the ADC, quantized to at least 32 steps from mute to the cap |
| REQ-SYS-060 | Show frequency, power step, key mode, speed, battery and transmit state outside menus | Announce the same items in Morse on demand (long press or menu command); transmit state on the LED |
| REQ-SYS-061 | Frequency characters at least 4.0 mm high | Retired |
| REQ-SYS-062 | Every setting within two menu levels from the status screen | Within two Morse-menu levels from menu entry, with R/N confirmation and a 20 s (TBR) time-out |
| REQ-SYS-063 | 5 W only after a step selection and a separate confirmation press | 5 W only after the Morse read-back and an R |
| REQ-SYS-066 | Guest lock set and released only by a held button combination followed by a confirmation | Keep: MENU and ALT held 3 s, then R on MENU (section 8.7) |
| REQ-SYS-067 | Display a distinct cause message within 1 s | Announce a distinct cause in Morse within 1 s (headphones) and an LED blink code (no headphones) |
| REQ-SYS-068, 069, 096, 171 | Show or display the ID reminder, separation reminder, low-battery warning, key-down time | Announce in Morse (and LED for low battery); timing values unchanged |
| REQ-SYS-070 | Indicate charge state whenever USB power is present | Retired (no in-radio charging; the MC1 indicates) |
| REQ-SYS-146 | Reserved enclosure position, controller input and display field for band control | Reserved enclosure position, controller input and Morse-menu entry |
| REQ-SYS-163 | Accept a key-input mode menu selection while any key input reads closed | Keep: the ALT-hold key-mode path uses the buttons only (section 8.7) |
| REQ-SYS-164 | Band crossing within 30 s at 2 rev/s of the knob | Any in-band frequency reached by Morse direct entry within 30 s (TBR) |
| REQ-SYS-165 | Displayed frequency legible at 0.5 m under 300 lux | Retired |
| REQ-SW-KEYER-014 | Element and space errors within +/-0.5 % or +/-0.2 ms at 50 display frames/s and 50 detents/s per encoder | Same error limits while the Morse menu sender, the pot scan and the FC0 frequency counter run at their maximum rates (no display, no encoder) |
| REQ-SW-KEYER-023 | Report each KEY inhibit onset and its cause to the display interface within 20 ms | Report to the Morse announcement and LED interface within 20 ms |
| REQ-SW-KEYER-032 | Hang of 3 to 30 dits of the displayed speed | Of the set speed |

**Frequency and receiver.**

| REQ | Current | Proposed |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-SYS-008, 009, REQ-TX-002 | Carrier 144.0012 to 147.9988 MHz (TBR); inhibit outside | Keep (TCXO fitted, D-17) |
| REQ-SYS-010 | +/-2.5 ppm (TBR) of the displayed frequency, -10 to +45 C, one year after calibration | Keep, "of the set frequency" (TG2520SMN +/-0.5 ppm over -40 to 85 C; aging not read, Low) |
| REQ-SYS-182, REQ-SYS-154 | Withhold or end RF within 100 ms unless an independent measurement agrees within 10 kHz; Fault-safe when the synthesizer is unlocked or more than 10 kHz off | **Keep both, on route R3 of `docs/design/analysis/frequency-budget.md`** (section 7.3, revision 6; D-17). Divider 74LVC1G80GV x3 (160 MHz minimum) into GPIN0; RP2350 FC0, interval 12 (4 ms) before PA_EN and 13 (8 ms) during transmission; the TCXO counted on GPIN1 in receive, ratio accepted within +/-67.5 ppm; healthy disagreement 4 518 Hz (interval 12) and 2 518 Hz (13) with the XOSC at 65 ppm; measured threshold 5.0 kHz (an SW-SAFE value, WP-PDR-35), margins 482 and 2 482 Hz, largest undetected error 9 518 Hz; fault detected and RF ended in 46 ms. The squaring stage that feeds GPIN1 is a lien (INSP-110 finding-25, WP-PDR-20): a stage that fails to count reads as DIED, so the fault is safe (no RF) but the radio would not transmit. No widening is proposed; the widening that the route without a TCXO would need (REQ-SYS-182 at 16.1 kHz or more, REQ-SYS-154 at 27.7 kHz or more) applies only if the TCXO becomes unavailable (section 10 revisit condition). HZ-008 K7 wording for R3: request to WP-PDR-16b (the frequency-budget note's section 5) |
| REQ-SYS-022 | MDS at most -140 dBm (TBR) | Keep the value; **not shown** at the TC-SYS-017 corner. With 2 + 3 + 4 (D-15) the A5 cascade gives -140.7 dBm nominal, -134.9 dBm at the TC-SYS-017 filter corner and -129.4 dBm at the stacked corner, all at 25 C (`rx-bpf-ts012.md` revision 4 section 8; run r11; TPM-005 Yellow on the CBE, Red at the corners). The front-end redesign of WP-PDR-19 (a lower-NF, higher-gain first stage, a lower-loss BPF1 or four-resonator BPF3, gain ahead of BPF3, or higher coil Q) must recover about 5.1 dB at the corner before PDR, and keep the image at 70 dB or more at the r13 corner; otherwise the owner rules a delta then. The MDS over REQ-SYS-114 is not analysed |
| REQ-SYS-023 (Goal) | MDS -142 dBm | Record as not met (nominal -140.7 dBm) |
| REQ-SYS-029 | 3 dB loss with a -60 dBm signal 2 kHz away | -65 dBm (TBR) or an accepted limitation (Si5351 close-in phase noise) |
| REQ-SYS-031 | RMDR at least 85 dB at 10 kHz | 78 dB (TBR); estimate 78 to 83 dB (adversarial C15) |
| REQ-SYS-032, 033 | Level range -120 to -20 dBm; 70 dB image and IF rejection | **Keep both (70 dB, TBR).** The design filter **2 + 3 + 4 at IF 8 MHz** (D-15) holds REQ-SYS-033 over REQ-SYS-114 (`rx-bpf-ts012.md` revision 4, `63122e7`; INSP-117 iteration 3 re-issue 1, reviewer APPROVED, `123f048`; run r13, `hardware/sim/rx-frontend/results/2026-09-29-r13-bpf-temperature/temperature_residual.png`): **worst case 75.37 dB, a margin of 5.37 dB**, at the design-input corner with the board at +70 C, tuned to 148.0 MHz, at the +/-0.3 % residual estimate (79.03 dB with the board at -10 C; 80.04 dB at the alignment temperature; 70.99 dB with both temperature-coefficient classes doubled). Residual limit over temperature **+/-0.64 %**; build alignment acceptance **+/-0.62 %** at 20 to 30 C (about +/-0.90 MHz), which also goes to REQ-SYS-144 below. The room-temperature limit (+/-0.95 %) does not hold over temperature, and revision 6's "+/-0.97 %" and "about 79.4 dB" are superseded (INSP-110 finding-26). Conditions of the verdict (note section 5): mode B C0G capacitors (coupling B, ends C tolerance), per-resonator alignment on the NanoVNA at 20 to 30 C, every internal port within VSWR 1.2 of 50 ohm (the J310 stages need matching networks), at most 0.03 pF stray per section, whole-chain antenna-to-mixer isolation of about 89 dB at 124 to 132 MHz, and coils in the +5 to +70 ppm/K class. Half-IF: the diode ring passes at -70 dBm with 34 to 37 dB margin (Medium confidence). A relaxation of REQ-SYS-033 is not proposed (the image band is the aeronautical AM band) |

**Transmitter and safety.**

| REQ | Current | Proposed |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-SYS-012 (KDR) | 5 W +/-1 dB (TBR) at 6.4 to 8.4 V | **5 W +1/-1.5 dB at the 6.4 V end (TBR).** The PA drive analysis (`pa-drive-ts012.md`, reviewer APPROVED) shows 3.97 W at 6.4 V in no steady key-down case: nominal 4.09 W, lowest corner 3.19 W (25 C, output filter at its median). With the drain-feed and VGG levers (the note's C2 and C3) the delta covers every key-down case at the LPF median (worst -1.36 dB) but not the LPF worst case (-2.51 dB). The owner decides between a firm delta and a bench check on the built unit (TC-SYS-011) with the delta as fallback (follow-on decision 2; PA drive analysis C8). The rationale's "PD54008L-E gives 4.5 to 5.0 W at 6 V" is replaced by the RA07M1317M values; revision 2's "3.97 to 4.57 W at 6.4 V" is withdrawn |
| REQ-SYS-013 | Survive 60 s of 5 W keying at 80 % duty into any load up to SWR 10:1, including open and short | Keep; closed on the module's specified load ruggedness (VSWR 20:1 at all phases at 9.2 V and 7 W without degradation, which covers 60 s at 80 % duty into 10:1), with no SWR fold-back (section 7.3; recorded in the A5 ADR) |
| REQ-SYS-017, 018, REQ-TX-009 to 011 | 25 uW; 60 dBc; harmonic filter values | Keep 017 and 018: met with the BOM values and catalog coils by 7.5 to 9.5 dB (017) and with either tolerance (018; D-14 adopts 2 % parts); `lpf-ts012.md`, reviewer APPROVED. **REQ-TX-009, 010, 011: 43, 35, 40 dB**, stated as attenuation at the harmonic less the passband loss at the carrier (LPF analysis section 7 item 4). The 0.5 dB passband-loss criterion becomes a design goal; the worst-case loss (1.76 dB for the 2 % build) is carried as the bound and the NanoVNA measures it on the unit. The binding harmonic case (5 W at 8.4 V) is a back-off state outside the module's guarantee, so the tinySA sweep confirms it. The transmit clock lines stay at risk (3 over 25 uW at the high estimate) until the tinySA sweep, which waits for the owner's purchase |
| REQ-SYS-014, 015, REQ-TX-005 | Raised-cosine 3 to 8 ms; 26 dB bandwidth at most 350 Hz; 10-to-90 % times within +/-10 % (TBR) of the command at the antenna port | Keep, implemented by the closed VGG envelope loop with the D-9 and D-10 items: every element after the first passes; the first element of an over meets REQ-TX-005 only at RSS, not at the worst-case sum of its estimated budget (`keying-ts012.md` section 5, reviewer APPROVED). REQ-TX-005 keeps +/-10 % (TBR). WP-PDR-22 reruns with circuit values, the fault cases and a simulated power-on |
| REQ-TX-014 | At most 1 uW (TBR) while TX_KEY is deasserted with PA_EN asserted and the exciter driven | **Restate** (follow-on decision 4): "... at most 1 uW (TBR) while TX_KEY is deasserted with PA_EN asserted and the synthesizer's transmit output running". The D-18 gate unpowers the GVA-84+ and clamps the VGG whenever TX_KEY is low, which gives about -61 dBm at key-up with CLK1 running (estimate, keying note path). That estimate rests on the driver being unpowered, which the gate as drawn may not achieve (INSP-110 finding-24, INSP-118 finding-9; fix due with the WP-PDR-22 key-up rerun, before the re-baseline CR carries the restatement). The module's VGG-off isolation is checked on receipt. **HZ-004 note for the CR:** REQ-TX-014 stays the transmitter half of REQ-SYS-120's two conditions; the restatement does not relax it, because TX_KEY removes both the drive and the gate bias in hardware, and the PA_EN half gates the same two points through the same gate, so a single stuck or corrupted line (TX_KEY, PA_EN or the permit flag behind it) produces no RF (HZ-004 K8; 07 section 14.2 row i). Routed to WP-PDR-16 and 17 (HZ-004 analysis) |
| REQ-SYS-156 | Fault-safe within 100 ms (TBR) of the forward-power reading leaving the plausible range | Keep; it is the bound on the open-loop ALC case (VGG at the pack-dependent clamp, D-9) and the mid-ramp check on a late relay contact (section 7.3, revision 3) |
| REQ-SYS-160, 161 | RF rise within 15 ms of a straight-key closure; constant lead-in of at most 12 ms | Keep (TBR closes here): lead-in 10 ms, set by the G5V-2 operate time of 7 ms maximum plus bounce and margin; 1 + 2 + 10 = 13 ms against 15 ms (section 7.3, adversarial R-1); the frequency check ends at t0 + 7 ms, before PA_EN (section 7.3, revision 6) |
| REQ-SYS-055, 120, 180, 092 | Hardware cutoff 7.5 to 13 s; two conditions; backstop 150 to 180 s; USB inhibit | Keep, all implemented on the envelope reference and VGG node. REQ-SYS-120's two conditions are TX_KEY (keyer) and **PA_EN** (a separate GPIO written only by the safe-state manager from its separately maintained PA-permit flag, 4.7 kohm pull-down), combined with the monostable Q in a hardware NAND that unpowers the GVA-84+ and clamps the VGG node (D-18); the clamp alone holds REQ-SYS-120 (INSP-110 finding-24). CLK1 enable is a firmware I2C action and is not the second condition. REQ-SYS-180 at risk from RC timer tolerance; if +/-9 % cannot hold, widen to 130 to 200 s (TBR) by CR (WP-PDR-26) |
| REQ-SYS-181 | End antenna-port RF within 100 ms, independently of firmware, when the PA heat-sink temperature exceeds 95 C +/-3 C | **Keep value and wording.** The sensor is the sink NTC (row 26) on LM393 #1, routed apart from the ADC path; it acts with the module channel at about 120 to 123 C against its 175 C rating (INSP-118 finding-2; thermal record `trips.csv`), so it is the backstop behind the REQ-SYS-118 inhibit. (Revision 6's tab-copper sensing point and wording change were for A4 and do not apply.) |
| REQ-SYS-083 | Independent cell over-voltage protection 4.25 to 4.30 V (TBR) from 0 to 45 C | S-8252AAO VCU 4.250 V, +/-25 mV from -10 to +60 C (datasheet Rev.4.0_00): 4.225 to 4.275 V, up to 25 mV under the window. Propose 4.225 to 4.30 V (TBR), reworded for the absent charger (the layer now backs up the external charger) |
| REQ-SYS-084 | Disconnect below 2.50 V +/-0.05 V (TBR) | At risk (revision 3, finding-15). S-8252AAO VDL 2.500 V: +/-0.050 V at Ta = +25 C only; -0.085 / +0.060 V over -40 to +85 C (datasheet Rev.4.0_00, electrical characteristics); no tighter band is published for -10 to +45 C, so the requirement holds only at 25 C. Propose 2.415 to 2.560 V (TBR) over the operating range, keeping the lower bound above the cell's discharge cut-off (to be read on the P28A sheet), or keep 2.45 to 2.55 V and record it at risk until the PDR protector-variant selection |
| REQ-SYS-085 | Discharge trip between 3 A and 10 A (TBR), independent of firmware | Keep. VDIOV 0.200 V +/-0.010 V across two AO3400A at 30 to 64 mohm total (typical cold to maximum at about 60 C and VGS 6 to 8 V, interpolated from the 25 C and 125 C datasheet values): 2.97 to 7.0 A. The 3 A floor is met to within 1 % at the hot maximum corner (at risk); the key-down load of about 2.0 A stays under the lowest trip |
| REQ-SYS-112 (KDR) | Tj at most 110 C at 45 C, continuous key-down at 5 W | **Fails as written**: A5 126.0 C (band +20.0 / -12.4 K, 113.6 to 145.9 C) with the section 8.14 design changes (`thermal-ts012.md` section 6, reviewer APPROVED). Proposed delta (owner, by CR; follow-on decision 1): a duty-limited corner, for example "at most 110 C at 50 % key-down duty in 13 s key-downs at 45 C" (A5 104.4 C, OPEN within its band), enforced by the REQ-SYS-118 inhibit; or a 25 C continuous corner (A5 106.1 C nominal, no band computed). Revision 4's "87 to 119 C" estimate is withdrawn. **REQ-SYS-118:** 85 C +/-3 C does not bound 110 C; proposed about 81 C at an NTC on the module flange contact face (D-6), or a firmware duty limit that refuses a new key-down above 79.4 C on a sink NTC, final value after the bench offset measurement (WP-PDR-28). Open thermal items that go with the delta (section 10 condition 3): the module case at about 106 C (105.7 C) against the Mitsubishi 90 C guidance in continuous sending; the long-session cells at 58.9 C against 55 C; the PETG face at 60.1 C against 60 C at the duty limit |
| REQ-SYS-113 | Hand-hold surfaces at most 48 C after 5 min at 5 W, 25 C | Keep; met only with the wrap-around guard (D-3): 32.3 C at 5 min (the end-face guard of revision 4 fails, 64.2 C); the set of surfaces is every reachable surface (CR-003 revision 3 reading); test-finger check in WP-PDR-27 |
| REQ-SYS-141 | Transmit monitor port, 40 dB +/-1 dB | Keep (baseline in revision 2) |
| REQ-SYS-183 | RF-off at most -57 dBm | Keep; designed out by the key-up sequence and the D-18 gate; tinySA check |

### 8.11 Owner price-check list (browser, no checkout)

1. Every Mouser row of section 8.3 (all aggregator reads), with its lifecycle flag, especially: Coilcraft 1812SMS-82NJLC stock (79), LM2940CT-5.0 stock (141), Adafruit 2045 (Mouser part 485-2045), TG2520SMN stock (1,810) and its output level on the datasheet (the D-17 squaring stage), 74LVC1G80GV; the 1812SMS value list, that 82 nH (the drive filter value) and the 68 and 82 nH G (2 %) grades are stocked (PA drive analysis C7; LPF analysis section 7 item 1), and the 47 nH value if the drive bandpass D-13 is adopted; the G5V-2 high-sensitivity 5 V coil price (D-5).
2. The estimated Mouser rows: KEMET C1206C390J1GACTU; the SMD passive list (Claude supplies the values from the schematic), including the BPF C0G 0805 parts in B tolerance (0.56, 0.75, 0.8 and 1.2 pF) and C tolerance (4.3 and 4.7 pF), all C0G (receiver note section 7 item 2; if B tolerance is not stocked at a listed price, the r08 to r10 and r13 corners are re-run with C tolerance); M3, M2.5 and M2.6 hardware and standoffs; knobs if needed.
3. The Mouser cart: merchandise total, shipping charge, the free-shipping threshold, and the tariff line.
4. RF Parts: RA07M1317M-501 stock and cart shipping to the owner's address (the USD 15 minimum is met by the module).
5. JLCPCB: the instant quote for the two designs (2-layer, 1.6 mm, HASL, 5 pcs each), the cheapest US shipping that prepays duty, the duty shown and whether it is charged on the boards or on the whole order.
6. 18650BatteryStore: cart shipping for 2 P28A and 1 XTAR MC1; the MC1 termination voltage and tolerance on its product sheet; whether the MC2 is back in stock.
7. Boyd 530002B02500G drawing: mass, a flat inner-channel face of at least 30 x 10 mm for the module flange with room for the screws, the 17.02 mm channel width and the 1.57 mm web behind it (D-1), and the fin layout (the thermal record read the drawing for revision 5; the owner confirms it on the page).
8. The DMP3099L-7 datasheet RDS(on) maximum at VGS -6 V and the Bourns MF-R300 R1max (the REQ-SYS-012 feed budget, at most 0.35 ohm; Claude can read them if the owner prefers).
9. Owner stock: through-hole resistor and capacitor assortment against the BOM value list (Claude supplies it), 24 AWG magnet wire quantity (about 3 m), the 2 m antenna's connector (SMA male) and band, the USB power adapter (5 V at 1 A or more for the MC1), the two potentiometers (values, shafts, knobs), and whether the resistor assortment is 1 % metal film (the VGG clamp divider and the D-7 pads). Cells and charger: answered, not owned (status note section 11).

### 8.12 What changes in the PDR work plan, CR-003 and CR-006

**PDR work plan (`docs/plan/pdr-work-plan.md`).** Revision 8 states the A5 changes. The plan's revision 3 and later carry them (its section 3.0 governs where it differs, section 3.0a gives each superseded or unwritten study its disposition, and section 10.6 maps these rows to WPs and sessions).

| WP or item | Change |
|---|---|
| WP-PDR-04, OD-04, OD-34 | The PCBWay email, the PCBWay instant quotes and the Inrad, KVG and Guerrilla RF requests are cancelled. They are replaced by the owner price-check list of section 8.11 and the ordering gate of section 8.4 (OD-42). No quote is requested from anyone |
| WP-PDR-19 (TS-001, superseded by TS-012) | The hand-matched 6-pole 500 Hz ladder is re-admitted; the Inrad and 24-bit ADC candidates are dropped; analog audio is kept; the diode-ring mixer on BN-43-202 cores is the baseline. The front-end filter is **2 + 3 + 4 at IF 8 MHz** (D-15; `rx-bpf-ts012.md` revision 4, INSP-117 APPROVED): REQ-SYS-033 held over REQ-SYS-114 at a worst case of 75.37 dB, alignment acceptance +/-0.62 % (limit +/-0.64 %). The image criterion is the one revision 5 adopted: at least 70 dB at the worst-case corner of the stated tolerance box, at every tuned frequency (100 kHz spacing), over REQ-SYS-114 after the stated alignment, with the port and stray limits stated; a Monte Carlo reported only as a yield; BPF1 loss judged in the REQ-SYS-022 cascade at the TC-SYS-017 corner. The half-IF passes with the ring (34 to 37 dB). Left: the front-end redesign for REQ-SYS-022 (A5 must recover about 5.1 dB at the TC-SYS-017 corner; TPM-005 Red there), which re-runs r13 and r11 for any new BPF3 and keeps the image at 70 dB or more at the r13 corner; the J310 port matching networks (condition 3); the written alignment procedure; `rx-cascade.md` on 2 + 3 + 4 |
| WP-PDR-20 (TS-007, superseded by TS-012; clock plan) | Adafruit Si5351A module with the TG2520SMN TCXO on XA; REQ-SYS-010 kept. Route R3 (D-17): FC0 on GPIN0 and GPIN1; a squaring stage that toggles at the TCXO's minimum output, powered in receive only (INSP-110 finding-25), and its effect on the 150.000 MHz spur line; the TCXO ratio's freshness over a long over (1 ppm allocation); the Si5351A PLL relock time at the changeover (1 ms allocation). Transmit clock plan (D-12, `spurs-ts012.md`, reviewer APPROVED, met by wording only): `clock_plan.py` rerun at clk_sys 96 MHz and the ADR-031 revision; the prescaler tap valid-clock run with the coax interface and the lowest CLK1 swing (1.66 Vpp); the feedforward PWM at 122 kHz added to the PWM rules. The three clock lines over 25 uW at the high estimate close at the tinySA sweep |

**CR-003 (enclosure, Submitted, held).** Needs a revision 4 before disposition: the printed PETG case stays the first and delivered enclosure, but (a) the heat sink sits wholly outside the case end wall with the module in its inner channel (D-1), the end wall facing it is a spare JLCPCB board (FR4, copper toward the sink) in a PETG frame 5 mm from the inner fin tips (D-2), a printed wrap-around finger guard covers the fins (end face 10 mm from the fin tips; top, bottom and profile ends 3 mm; 2 mm slotted grille; D-3), no PETG part touches the sink, and inside the case the module leads and the RF board sit in a vented PA bay with 5 cm2 slots top and bottom, behind a printed double-wall bulkhead, with 2 cm2 main-bay slots and none on top (IPX2; D-4) and the cells at the far end; (b) no conductive coating is bought for build 1 and REQ-SYS-177 is deferred; (c) the PCBWay CNC fallback and the 6061 port block are outside the USD 300 maximum, so the fallback becomes a second printed iteration (free filament) or an owner cost decision; (d) the legend and jack markings stay in relief (OD-38 route (a)); (e) the TS-011 PC-class filament condition is re-run for the A5 heat layout on the thermal record (hottest PETG 66.0 C continuous at the guard, 60.1 C at the duty limit on the bulkhead's bay side, the FR4 end wall at 74.9 C continuous against its 105 C limit) before it is asked of the owner; and (f) REQ-SYS-104, 106, 109, 113 and 124 follow section 8.10.

**CR-006 (build sequence, Submitted, held).** Its reading (one assembled unit first) stands, but its PCBWay content does not: (a) five bare boards of each of two designs come from JLCPCB and the owner assembles one unit; (b) REQ-SYS-147, NGO-028 and MOE-007 take the first-unit USD 200 target and USD 300 maximum instead of USD 610 amortized over the first build; (c) NGO-027 changes from PCBWay turnkey to JLCPCB bare boards with owner hand assembly; (d) questions Q2 (PCBWay assembled count), Q3 (unit-cost options) and Q8 (unused turnkey parts) become moot; (e) the TC-SYS-025 and MOE-001 and 002 content (the Baofeng source and the matched second station, status note section 5) is unaffected. Recommendation: fold both CR revisions' requirement content and the section 8.10 deltas into the re-baseline CR (WP-PDR-53), with its section 6 impact review before the owner's disposition (OD-40).

| # | Question | Recommendation |

| Q1 | **(Revision 6.) Choose A4 with its now-required conditions and A5's diode-ring mixer (score 300; USD 208.51 planning, 272.17 worst; End-of-Life PA with a spare; the TCXO, one flagged part; half the heat in the case) or A5 (score 270; USD 248.83 planning, 316.51 worst, over USD 300 before the guards and USD 0.17 inside after them; module PA, TCXO, diode ring; twice the heat in the case)?** Both need the same REQ-SYS-112 delta and a REQ-SYS-012 delta (A4's larger), both pass 97.307(e) with the BOM filter, neither shows the receiver MDS at the tolerance corner, and both now carry the same frequency check (TCXO, route R3) and PA_EN gating. A4's REQ-SYS-181 sensor moves to the PA tab, where its margin to the device rating is about 8 K at the all-adverse corner (A5's about 52 K). **A2 (305, not analysed) scores 5 points above the recommended A4** and is presented with them (06 section 14.5): it keeps in-radio USB charging, but carries three flagged parts, a new-old-stock PA pair whose RD07MUS2B is "no longer available for export", an LCSC-only charger chain and the HZ-002 charger in the box, and half its cells are unanalysed estimates of the kind the analyses moved down for both finalists. Please also say whether you agree to keep A2 out of the choice set, or want its analysis run first (about two days of analysis and review, no money; section 6 revision 6 item 4). | **A4 with the TCXO and the diode-ring mixer (U3), and keep A2 out of the choice set.** Net of the re-scoring since revision 4, A5 fell 65 points and the recommended A4 40. Every discriminating analysis moved against A5 (heat and the junction range, the drive window, the low-pack power, the first keyed element, the cost of the added parts), while the guard envelope that both need removed A4's size advantage and the TCXO that A4 now needs removed its no-flagged-part advantage. The recommended A4 leads A5 by 30 points and is first in every weight run between the two; no single uncertain score closes it. A4 without the ring leads by only 15, so the ring is part of the recommendation (sections 1, 6, 8 and 8.2) |

Revisit for a later build |

Asked with the re-issued sections 8.1 to 8.12, after Q1 |

**Follow-on decisions after Q1 (revision 5; not asked now, each put to the owner with its record when the chosen finalist's sections are re-issued):**
1. REQ-SYS-112: the duty-limited corner (or a 25 C continuous corner), by CR; REQ-SYS-118 setpoint about 77 C (A4) or 81 C (A5) at a PA-case NTC (thermal analysis sections 8 and 9.2).
2. REQ-SYS-012 at the 6.4 V end: a firm delta, or a bench check on the built unit with the delta as fallback (PA drive analysis C4 and C8).
3. LPF: the BOM values with 2 % parts; 0.5 dB loss as a goal with the worst case carried; REQ-TX-009, 010, 011 values (LPF analysis section 7).
4. REQ-TX-014: the drive gating in the key path (revision 6: in hardware, D-18) and the restated condition with its HZ-004 note (section 8.10; keying analysis section 8 item 4, option (a)), after WP-PDR-16 and 17 and INSP-118 iteration 2 have seen it.
5. Receiver front end: revision 6 takes 2 + 3 + 4 at IF 8 MHz (owner authorization of INSP-117 iteration 4, status note 2026-09-29 section 2); the MDS redesign in WP-PDR-19 follows the note's revision 4 (receiver analysis section 7).
6. For A5 only: the REQ-SYS-144 select-on-test pad and its reading method (PA drive analysis C1). (Revision 6: the A4 TCXO add-back AB2 is withdrawn; the TCXO is required.)
7. (Revision 6.) REQ-SYS-181 wording for A4: the sensing point of TS-011 rule 14 (the PA tab copper) in place of "PA heat-sink temperature", value unchanged (section 8.10).

## 9. Dissent

**The pre-order analysis records and their review verdicts (table R5-1; revision 6 cites the final verdicts).** Every record is developer evidence (Python checkers without a TV record) with LTspice runs through the accredited wrapper (ACC-LTSPICE-001). "Reviewer APPROVED" means no Major finding is open (PDR plan rule C1); the record verdict of those three is held at NEEDS CHANGES only because the analysis checklist template is still on the `cr/CR-012` branch (lead SE convention of 2026-09-27), not for a finding. Where a re-review is pending, the figures used here are those of the revision that answers the open finding; section 10 names a change on re-review as a revisit condition.

**Adversarial refutations of revision 2 (2026-09-27) and their disposition in revision 3.**

| Claim | Verdict | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R-1 T/R key-down sequence ("relay to TX, 5 ms settle, GVA bias on, CLK1 on, then the envelope ramp", section 7.3) | Refuted, not routed (major) | Accepted. Omron G5V-2 datasheet read by the author: operate 7 ms maximum, release 3 ms maximum (bounce 0.3 to 0.5 ms from its graph). Settle raised to 10 ms (inside REQ-SYS-161's 12 ms and REQ-SYS-160's 15 ms); the reference and VGG clamps held and the integrator parked at 0 V by an idle bias until the ramp; a mid-ramp detector plausibility check (REQ-SYS-156); the 3.47 V clamp bounds any residual step. Routed: WP-PDR-22 now models the relay (closing at 7.5 ms and, as a fault, at 12 ms) with pass criteria, and WP-PDR-23 carries the sequence timing with pass criteria and closes the REQ-SYS-160 and 161 TBRs. No cost. A4 takes the same sequence |
| R-2 "Heat outside the case" (C8 score 3 for A5; sections 1, 4.2, 7.3) | Refuted, not routed (major) | Accepted: the claim was wrong and is withdrawn from sections 1, 4.2 and 7.3. A vented PA bay behind a double-wall bulkhead, the cells moved to the far end of the main board and the cell 60 C trip as the backstop (no cost; +5 mm length, +3 to 5 g). Author's lumped estimate after the change 54 to 56 C cell-bay air at the 45 C continuous corner (Low). Routed: WP-PDR-28 criteria for cell surface, main-bay air and every PETG surface, before the order, with a bench check. New HZ-007 cell-heating risks in 7.1 for A3, A4 and A5 (Red by the safety override), merged into RSK-007. C8-A5 stays 3 on the anchor "heat near PETG needing design controls", Low; its -1 move is now one of the five that change the top rank |

**INSP-110 iteration 3 (on revision 3 at d5a3058; reviewer APPROVED, Minor liens) and their disposition in revision 4.**

| Finding | Severity | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| finding-14 | Minor | Fixed. The clamp is sized on the LM2940's 4.75 to 5.25 V over temperature (TI SNVS769J section 6.5, re-read by the author 2026-09-27) and 1 % resistors: a 0.654 divider of E24 values (for example 2.7 k over 5.1 k) gives a ratio of 0.6493 to 0.6584, so VGG is at most 3.46 V and at least 3.08 V, before op-amp headroom, the module's gate current and LDO dropout, which only lower it. The resistors come from the owner's assortment if it is 1 % (gate item 9), else from the E2 assortment-gap allowance, so no total changes. The WP-PDR-22 deck models the LDO range and dropout, the resistor tolerance, the op-amp headroom and the module's VGG input current; the lower low-end clamp is carried into the REQ-SYS-012 check (sections 7.3, 8.1, 8.10, 8.12). A reference independent of the 5 V bus was not taken: it adds a part, and the 1 % divider meets the 3.5 V condition |
| finding-17 | Minor | Fixed, by the reviewer's second option: the A5 envelope-loop row (6, Yellow) is module-specific with no A4 counterpart, so it is counted against U1 in section 8.2. With it alone the total is 10 points at USD 4.71 per point; with the R-4 junction change below it is 6 points at USD 7.85, and U1 nets -2. Sections 1, 6 item 4, 8 and Q1 restated |
| finding-18 | Minor | Fixed. Status note section 11 is recorded in sections 1, 2 and 8.9 (EX-4, EX-13): cells and charger not owned and priced; instruments outside the radio's cap under the separate USD 300 equipment cap, with the tinySA Ultra (if not yet bought), the thermocouple and an SMA-to-BNC adapter for the owner's BNC dummy load counted there; EX-13 no longer calls the tinySA committed. Q2 and Q4 are closed as answered, and gate item 9 no longer asks about cells. Section 13 is recorded: LTspice accredited (ACC-LTSPICE-001) in sections 2, 3.4 and 6 item 6. No cost or score changes |
| X-10 | Cross item | The brief for this revision was again cut off, inside the second adversarial item ("The flange face is also un"); any refutation after R-4 was not delivered and is not answered here. The lead SE should confirm the list against the adversarial report |

**Adversarial refutations of revision 3 (2026-09-27) and their disposition in revision 4.** The label R-4 is this study's; the brief labelled R-3 and left the second item unlabelled.

| Claim | Verdict | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R-3 (section 7.3, non-harmonic spurs): "USB is off in transmit by the VBUS inhibit (REQ-SYS-092), which removes the 48 MHz x 3 product" | Refuted, not routed (major) | Accepted and withdrawn. The RP2350 ADC needs clk_adc at 48 MHz ("Must be 48MHz", RP2350 datasheet clock table, as cited in `docs/design/analysis/clock-plan.md` and ADR-031 item 5), and A4 and A5 read the ADC in transmit (the REQ-SYS-156 and mid-ramp checks), so 3 x 48 = 144.000 MHz stays in transmit, on the 12 MHz x 12 line, 50 kHz from a 144.050 MHz carrier and behind about 45 dB of gain. The author also found that the 125 MHz transmit system clock (itself in the passband; ADR-031 uses 150 MHz), the 25 MHz reference's 5th and 6th harmonics (125 and 150 MHz) and the /8 prescaler's 7/8 and 9/8 products (126 to 130 and 162 to 167 MHz) lie inside the low-pass passbands. Routed, at no cost, to a pre-order transmit clock-plan item in WP-PDR-20 (every transmit clock with its lines from 118 to 175 MHz; an estimated level at the GVA-84+ input against about -61 dBm for 25 uW and -68 dBm for 60 dBc; the transmit clk_sys reconciled with ADR-031; the ADC clock in transmit chosen), then the tinySA sweep. Spur risk rows updated for A4 and A5; scores unchanged (12, Red) |
| R-4 (section 7.3): junction "bound" of 103 C (109 C extreme) | Refuted, routed to WP-PDR-28 | Accepted. The figure used the catalog free-air 2.6 K/W with an arbitrary +30 %, referred the whole sink to 45 C although part of the fins and the inner face sit in the vented bay (near 60 C by revision 3's own estimate), and ignored the guard 3 mm from the fin tips; the flange face and profile are unread. With about half the fin area effective (5 K/W) the case reaches 99 C (over the Mitsubishi 90 C guidance) and stage 2 about 119 C (over 110 C). The figure is restated as an estimate of 87 to 119 C; REQ-SYS-112 is at risk for A5 as for A4; the A5 junction risk rises from 8 (Yellow) to 12 (Red); C8 for A5 falls from 3 to 2 on the estimate-range rule A4 is scored on (now stated in section 3.1), which puts A4 first (340 against 335); section 6 re-run. WP-PDR-28 now models the fin split, bay air, guard clearance and flange face, with the fin-orientation and guard-clearance levers before the duty-limit fallback. No cost |

| Author (revision 4) | 2026-09-27 | The ranking between A4 and A5 is not robust (section 6); revision 4 moves A4 ahead by 5 points on the C8-A5 correction (R-4), and the owner's valuation of risk against the USD 200 target decides it | Both presented together (06 section 14.5), sections 1, 8 and 8.13 Q1 |

The author holds that the recommended A4 configuration (with the ring, 300) is the right choice between the finalists (30 points, first in every weight run) and that A2's lead is an evidence asymmetry plus a supply and HZ-002 risk the matrix does not score, but that the process now requires the owner's concurrence on the choice set | A4 with the TCXO and the ring recommended; A2 presented with it and the owner asked to confirm the choice set or order the A2 analysis (sections 6 revision 6 item 4, 8 and Q1) |

## 10. Decision

Recorded in revision 7 (2026-09-29). Sections 1 to 9 are the report as it went to the owner (revision 6); no score is changed by the decision.

- **Decision:** **Alternative A5** ("hybrid H1", sections 3.2 and 8): the Mitsubishi RA07M1317M-501 module PA with the Epson TG2520SMN TCXO, the diode-ring mixer and a spare GVA-84+, with the common items of section 8 and the revision 6 design items D-1 to D-18 of section 8.14, as the hand-built design of the first cwht. Decided **2026-09-29**. The study recommended A4 with the TCXO and A5's diode-ring mixer (U3), 300 against A5's 270, with A2 (305, not analysed) presented with them (section 8.13 Q1; 06 section 14.5). The owner chose other than the recommendation. The owner's choice among the three options put to her (A4, A5, or analyse A2 first; status note section 4) also confirms the choice set as A4 and A5, so the A2 analysis of section 6 revision 6 item 4 is not run and the revisit condition "the owner declines to confirm the A4 and A5 choice set" did not trigger (cross item X-R6-2).
- **Decided by:** Robin (owner, Decision Authority), in chat on 2026-09-29, transcribed verbatim in `docs/plan/status/status-2026-09-29.md` section 4 (`f35eb78`) and section 5 (`13176ee`). The decision was made between reviews, so the resulting ADR is the decision memo and states that the approval was given in chat and transcribed (06 section 14.5; charter section 2).
- **What was put to the owner:** section 4 of the status note: the decision in plain language with three options (1 choose A4, recommended; 2 choose A5; 3 analyse A2 first) and the revision 6 figures (A4 with the TCXO and ring USD 208.51 planning, 272.17 worst; A5 USD 248.83 planning, 316.51 worst, 299.83 after guards G1 and G2). Section 5: the A5 parts lifecycle check (`f193784`) and A5's trade-offs in plain terms: the costs and the ordering gate; about twice A4's heat in the case, the long-session cells at 58.9 C against 55 C, the duty-limited PETG face at its 60 C limit on a 45 C day and the module case at about 106 C against the 90 C guidance in continuous sending; 4.1 to 6.8 W for a typical unit, the worst-case unit under 3.97 W at 6.4 V. The study presented was revision 6 with its Dissent section (section 9) and the reviews of revision 6, INSP-110 iteration 3 re-issue 2 and INSP-118 iteration 2 (`ff2fd8c`, `c5e4930`; reviewer and assurance verdicts APPROVED, record verdicts held at NEEDS CHANGES for their Minor liens).
- **Rationale as stated by the owner (verbatim):** section 4: "This is a good write up. I'm leaning towards A5 if it doesn't use outdated components". Section 5, after the lifecycle report: "A5".
- **Lead SE reading of the rationale (Claude, not the owner's words):**
  1. **The owner's condition.** The owner made the choice turn on one condition: that the design use no outdated components. The study did not weight parts lifecycle as a criterion: it folded sourcing into M2, C5 and the section 7.1 risks, with RSK-038 (stock and end of life) as the carrier (section 3.1, "Other criteria considered"). The owner's condition therefore acts as a screen the owner applied at the decision, on top of the matrix. It is recorded here as the owner's rationale, not as a re-scoring (06 section 14.5; revision 7 changes no criterion, weight or score).
  2. **A4 fails the condition.** A4's PA, the NXP AFT05MS004NT1, is End of Life (INSP-110 iteration 1, one of its three Major findings on revision 1; A4 carried a spare device in its order for that reason, section 1). A2, not analysed, would not meet it either: its RD07MUS2B pair is new-old stock, "no longer available for export" (section 8.13 Q1). A5 is the finalist whose PA is in current production (Mitsubishi "Active", RF Parts "In Stock", 2026-09-27).
  3. **The lifecycle check.** The owner's condition was answered for every A5 part, not only the module: `docs/research/a5-parts-lifecycle-2026-09-29.md` (`f193784`) reads 39 of 39 entries Active on maker and distributor pages (section 8.3, the other-seller lines, row E1 and the row E5 logic parts), with two conflicting sources resolved by the maker (Omron G5V-2 in production; AOS AO3400A in full production) and one ordering note: the 2N3904 is bought as 2N3904BU (other suffixes Obsolete). Where only a distributor or aggregator snippet was read, the entry is Low confidence.
  4. **What the owner accepts with A5.** The owner was shown, and accepted, what the recommendation weighed against A5: 30 points below the recommended A4; USD 40.32 more at planning (248.83 against 208.51); a worst case over the USD 300 maximum before the guards; about twice A4's PA heat in the case with the open thermal items below. A5 keeps its advantages as scored: C4 (the module removes the hand match) and C5 (vendor harmonic and stability data at 6 W and 7.2 V), and a REQ-SYS-181 backstop about 52 K from the device rating against A4's 8 K.
- **Conditions that come with A5 (each from the study; the order and the re-baseline depend on them):**
  1. **Ordering gate (section 8.4).** Nothing is ordered until the owner's browser reads of the four carts and the JLCPCB quote are in, and Claude recomputes the planning and worst totals with 15 % contingency on every line. The order proceeds only if the recomputed worst case is at or below USD 300. The revision 6 stacked worst case is USD 316.51; guard **G1** (free Mouser shipping, up to -9.20) and **G2** (the owner's knobs and hardware, up to -7.48) give 299.83, USD 0.17 inside. **G3** (RF board folded into a 70 x 100 mm main board: -2.30 plus one board's duty) and **G4** (drop the spare GVA-84+: -3.44) are ready, in that order. **G6** (revert the PA to A4's AFT05) would bring back the End-of-Life part the owner's condition excludes; the lead SE reads it as no longer available without a new owner decision, so if the worst case is still over USD 300 after G1 to G4, the owner decides between not ordering and a change that reopens this study (section 14.6). The spare module (AB-A, Q7) is decided at the gate, only if the worst case with it stays at or below USD 300.
  2. **Requirement deltas (section 8.10) go to the re-baseline CR.** The A5 rows of section 8.10 (every row not marked A4) are carried by the re-baseline CR, which appends the owner inputs as SI ids; A4-only readings (the 30 ppm frequency rows, the A4 REQ-SYS-181 sensing wording) do not apply. Among them: **REQ-SYS-012** (KDR), the A5 low-pack delta, 5 W +1/-1.5 dB at the 6.4 V end (TBR), which is needed for the typical module and covers every key-down case at the LPF median (worst -1.36 dB) but not the LPF worst case (-2.51 dB); the owner still decides between a firm delta and a bench check on the built unit with the delta as fallback (follow-on decision 2, PA drive analysis C8). **REQ-SYS-112** (KDR): the duty-limited corner, with the REQ-SYS-118 inhibit on the flange NTC at about 81 C (follow-on decision 1; D-6). **REQ-TX-014**: the restatement with its HZ-004 note (follow-on decision 4). The UI, charging and protection rows of section 8.10 and the exceptions of section 8.9 go with them.
  3. **Open thermal items (thermal analysis `thermal-ts012.md`, section 7.3 table R5-2; PDR design work, WP-PDR-28 bench):** the long-session cells at the duty limit reach **58.9 C against the 55 C criterion** (FAIL outside its band, under the 60 C trip); the PETG face at the duty limit reaches **60.1 C against its 60 C limit** (FAIL inside its band; 66.0 C in continuous sending, outside it); the module case reaches **about 106 C (105.7 C) against the Mitsubishi 90 C guidance** in continuous sending. The relay ambient (67.1 C against 65 C) and the in-situ sink measurement (at most 5.6 K/W for A5) stay with them. None of these is closed by the decision; each is a PDR design item or a revisit condition below.
  4. **Owner questions still open after Q1 (section 8.13):** Q5 (the flagged TCXO and the surface-mount list), Q6 (the wrap-around guard envelope, about 167 x 74 x 52 mm for A5), Q7 (the spare module, at the gate), and follow-on decisions 1 to 6; each is put to the owner with the re-issued A5 sections, not decided here.
- **Design items fixed for A5 (section 8.14; the A5 values apply where an item gives two):**
  - **D-15, receiver band-pass: 2 + 3 + 4 resonators at IF 8 MHz**, per `docs/design/analysis/rx-bpf-ts012.md` **revision 4** (`63122e7`; INSP-117 iteration 3 re-issue 1, reviewer APPROVED, `123f048`). Its figures, which replace the revision 6 figures where this section cites them: REQ-SYS-033 over REQ-SYS-114 PASS with a worst case of **75.37 dB** (margin 5.37 dB, hot end at 148.0 MHz, +/-0.3 % residual estimate); residual limit over temperature **+/-0.64 %**; alignment acceptance **+/-0.62 %** at 20 to 30 C (the revision 6 "+/-0.97 %" is a room-temperature limit that does not hold over temperature); whole-chain antenna-to-mixer isolation about 89 dB; with the conditions of the note's section 5 (mode B parts, per-resonator alignment, internal ports within VSWR 1.2, at most 0.03 pF stray per section). REQ-SYS-022 is not shown: A5 nominal MDS -140.7 dBm, -134.9 dBm at the TC-SYS-017 filter corner (TPM-005 Red); the WP-PDR-19 MDS redesign follows the note (follow-on decision 5). Elsewhere in this study the revision 6 receiver figures ("about 79.4 dB", "+/-0.97 %", the review state of table R5-1) stay as they were presented, under INSP-110 finding-26 (lien, due before the re-baseline CR). **Revision 8:** the note's figures are carried into sections 8.3, 8.10, 8.11, 8.12 and D-15, and the revision 6 figures in sections 1, 6, 7.1, 7.3 and 9 are marked superseded in place (the finding-26 fix, for the INSP-110 delta to verify).
  - **D-17, frequency verification on route R3:** the TG2520SMN TCXO on the Si5351A's XA (the module crystal removed), through its own 10 nF and a buffer powered only in receive to GPIN1; the /8 prescaler to GPIN0; FC0 interval 12 before PA_EN and 13 during transmission; threshold 5.0 kHz, margin 482 Hz; the check ends at t0 + 7 ms, before PA_EN. REQ-SYS-182 and REQ-SYS-154 hold as written; guard G5 stays withdrawn.
  - **D-18, PA permit:** a PA_EN GPIO (4.7 kohm pull-down) written only by the safe-state manager; a 74LVC1G10-class NAND of TX_KEY, PA_EN and the cutoff monostable Q drives the clamp FET on the VGG node and the gate of the P-FET that alone supplies the GVA-84+ (with D-11: CLK1 enable is not a condition of REQ-SYS-120). **Open lien:** the gate's supply rail, its level interface to the 5 V P-FET and its unpowered state are not stated; as drawn the DMP3099L may not turn off (VGS about -1.7 V against a threshold of -1.0 to -2.1 V), and A5's -60.6 dBm key-up level rests on the driver being unpowered (INSP-118 finding-9, INSP-110 finding-24, one fix closes both; due with the WP-PDR-22 key-up rerun, before the re-baseline CR carries the REQ-TX-014 restatement). REQ-SYS-120 is not affected: the clamp FET holds the gate bias at 0 V on its own.
  - The other items apply as section 8.14 states them for A5: D-1 to D-5 (sink outside the end wall, FR4 end wall, wrap-around guard, slots, relay hold); D-6 (REQ-SYS-118 NTC on the flange contact face, about 81 C; the REQ-SYS-181 backstop on the sink); D-7 (select-on-test drive pad); D-8 (CLK1 coax); D-9 (pack-dependent VGG clamp); D-10 (keying loop items 1 to 10, low-pack setpoint limit 4.0 W); D-11; D-12 (transmit clock plan); D-13 and D-14 (drive bandpass, output LPF); D-16 (instruments, outside the radio's cap).
- **Records produced:** this revision 7 of TS-012 (committed alone). To follow, not produced here: the ADR for the A5 decision (the decision memo between reviews); the re-issue of the A5 sections 8.1 to 8.12 with the finding-26 receiver figures (**produced: revision 8**, committed alone); the risk register's `trade_study_ids` and `adr_ids` updates; the revisions of CR-003 and CR-006 for A5; the re-baseline CR with the section 8.10 deltas; the revised PDR work plan (status note section 5, "Next").
- **Revisit conditions (revision 5):** the verified ordering-gate worst case exceeds USD 300 after guards G1 to G4 (G5 withdrawn in revision 6; now likely for A5; G6 is the A5-to-A4 step); RF Parts shows the RA07M1317M-501 out of stock at the gate (A5); a re-review of the PA drive, keying or receiver record (table R5-1) changes a figure this study scores on, in particular A5's low-pack power (C5), the first-element keying window (C5) or the receiver configuration cost (E5); INSP-112 holds that the section 3.1 rule reads A5's thermal range as spanning 110 C (C8-A5 back to 2: A5 285, A4 still first); the A4 match run in LTspice (PA drive C5) shows a drain efficiency under about 50 % (the A4 inhibit setpoint then falls toward 60 C and its junction band widens) or an output that changes C5-A4; the in-situ sink measurement gives more than 5.6 K/W (A5) or 6.5 K/W (A4) (thermal section 9.1 item 2), which lowers the allowed duty; the owner declines the REQ-SYS-112 duty-limited delta (then neither finalist meets a KDR and the study is reopened); the WP-PDR-19 front-end redesign cannot show REQ-SYS-022 at the TC-SYS-017 corner with either finalist; the tinySA sweep shows a spur or harmonic over 25 uW that no named change removes; the feed-resistance reads exceed 0.35 ohm at 25 C.
- **Revisit conditions added in revision 6:** the PA drive and keying re-reviews (INSP-114 and INSP-116 iteration 3) changed no figure scored here, so that condition did not trigger for them; INSP-117 iteration 4 (2 + 3 + 4 over temperature) changes an image or MDS figure or the E5 (a) cost; the bench NTC-offset measurement shows more than about 18.8 K between the A4 tab and its NTC-2 reading at 6.9 W (the A4 tab cut-off would then reach the AFT05's 150 C; a lower A4 REQ-SYS-181 threshold by CR); the Si5351A PLL relock time exceeds the 1 ms allocation by more than the 3 ms between the check and the ramp, or the FC0 known-clock check fails its 560 Hz limit (the check moves to interval 13 and the lead-in to 11.5 ms); WP-PDR-20 cannot keep the TCXO ratio fresh over a long over within the 1 ppm allocation; the owner declines to confirm the A4 and A5 choice set (the A2 analysis of section 6 revision 6 item 4 is then run before the decision); the TCXO becomes unavailable at the gate (both finalists then need the REQ-SYS-182 and REQ-SYS-154 deltas of section 7.3, revision 6).
- **Revisit conditions added in revision 7:** (a) **Triggered, no score effect:** the revision 6 condition on INSP-117 iteration 4 triggered (note revision 4, `63122e7`; INSP-117 X-R4-1): the image figure changed (75.37 dB worst case over REQ-SYS-114) and D-15's alignment limit changed (+/-0.62 %); the filter, its parts and its MDS figures are common to both finalists, so no score moves (INSP-110 finding-26). (b) Any A5 part is found not Active (maker status End of Life, Not Recommended for New Designs or Obsolete) at the ordering gate: the owner's condition is then not met, and the owner is asked before anything is ordered. (c) The worst case is over USD 300 after G1 to G4: G6 conflicts with the owner's condition, so the owner decides (condition 1 above). (d) The WP-PDR-28 bench or the re-issued thermal work cannot bring the long-session cells, the PETG face or the module case inside their limits with a no-cost or gate-affordable change: the thermal items then go to the owner with the REQ-SYS-112 delta.
- **Lessons learned:** the decisive condition in the owner's choice, parts lifecycle status, was not a criterion of this study: it was folded into M2, C5 and RSK-038, so the matrix could not show it. Candidate entry for `docs/lessons-learned.md`: a design-to-cost study that selects parts screens lifecycle status (Active at the maker, with its source) as a mandatory criterion, or asks the owner at opening whether it is one. The entry is appended with the ADR commit; this revision commits the trade study alone.

## Appendix A. Supporting analysis

- **Literature and research search:** claude-context `search_code` on `/Users/robinonsay/rust/cwht` first (charter section 11 rule 1), queries on the adversarial check, REQ-SYS-033 and REQ-SYS-012, the RA07M1317M research and the keying-envelope requirements; then reads of known paths. Web: WebSearch and WebFetch of public product, aggregator, datasheet and shipping pages listed in section 11; Mouser, DigiKey and some catalog PDFs blocked or timed out.
- **Previous related decisions and dissent:** TS-001 (SRR decision 54 and the ladder pruning), TS-007, TS-011 (M8 PETG finding), ADR-007, SI-028, SI-031.
- **Detailed analysis:** the roll-up, the matrix and the sensitivity run were computed with short Python scripts in the author's scratchpad (not committed): 16 weight perturbations, every Low-cell perturbation and the joint adverse case, reproduced in section 6. The thermal and power figures of section 7.3 are hand calculations from the datasheet values named there. No LTspice deck exists yet for A4 or A5; the drive chain, envelope loop, LPF, BPF, half-IF and headphone ceiling decks are WP-PDR-19, 21, 22 and 25 products.
- **Revision 5 analysis:** the roll-up with row E5, the matrix, the 16 weight runs, every Low-cell move, every combination of up to three moves between A4 and A5, the joint adverse cases and the hybrids were computed with short Python scripts in the author's scratchpad (not committed; the TS is committed alone). The script first reproduces every revision 4 figure of section 6. No new simulation was run for this revision: every engineering figure comes from the six analysis records.
- **Revision 6 analysis:** the roll-ups with the required TCXO and E5 (g), the matrix, the 16 weight runs, every Low-cell move and every combination of up to three moves between the recommended A4 and A5 and between A2 and A4, the joint adverse cases and the named readings were computed with a Python script in the author's scratchpad (not committed; the TS is committed alone), which first reproduces every revision 5 figure of section 6. The frequency figures were re-run with the committed `freq_budget.py` functions; the A4 tab cut-off junction is hand arithmetic on the thermal record's `inhibit.csv` offsets and the AFT05 datasheet. No new simulation was run.
- **Decision metrics:** opened, recommended and revised the same day; six alternatives (one pruned tree of seven more); eight enhancing and five mandatory criteria; criteria revised once (revision 2: M1, C1, C2, C8 and the weights, after the owner's section 10 answers); one score corrected in revision 3 (C7-A5, 4 to 3) and the sensitivity re-run (the script reproduces the revision-2 figures before the change); in revision 4 the C8 estimate-range rule stated, one score corrected (C8-A5, 3 to 2), which changes the top rank, and the sensitivity re-run with the same script (which reproduces every revision-3 figure before the change). In revision 5 ten cells re-scored on the analysis evidence (C1-A5, C2-A2, C2-A4, C5-A3, C5-A5, C6-A4, C6-A5, C8-A2, C8-A3 and C8-A5), row E5 added to every roll-up, the top rank confirmed as A4 and made robust between the finalists. In revision 6 one cell re-scored (C3-A4 5 to 3, the required TCXO), row E5 item (g) added, the recommended configuration (A4 + U3) scored in the matrix, and the verdict restated as Not robust with A2 first on the matrix.

## Appendix B. Revision 1 results (superseded, kept for history)

Revision 1 (text at 5c16930) worked to a firm USD 200 cap "all in", with no owned stock beyond perfboard and potentiometers. It scored A1 to A4 with weights C1 25, C2 10, C3 15, C4 10, C5 15, C6 10, C7 5, C8 10 and recommended A4 (340 of 500; A1 255 and excluded at M4, A3 255, A2 215), with capped totals of USD 167.95 low, 190.29 planning and 212.64 high, the high case held under USD 200 by guard G1 (the owner's own copper wire, 197.47). INSP-110 then found three Major errors in its basis (EOL PA, no sales tax line, prescaler above its rating) and the adversarial check refuted three of its claims (C2, C7, C19). The owner's section 10 answers moved the cap to a USD 200 target with a USD 300 maximum, excluded sales tax and added owned stock, which led to this revision.
